# Chapter Summaries

## Volume 01 — The Permission Fault

### Chapter 0001 — The Door That Owes Nothing

Jonas replaces a worn River Stacks relay, then reaches Saint Orra bridge as an ambulance is rejected by conflicting east/west requests. He uses a 25-minute attended bypass rather than a suppression lead, serves the ambulance, and records an unresolved permission conflict. The old I. Vey seal and a downstream west-clinic request establish the service chain; Jonas remains Stage 0 and wants both permanent status and fuller archive clearance.

### Chapter 0002 — A Borrowed Key

At the west clinic, Sanaa needs Alma Reyes admitted while the gate rejects a valid care claim because the night-watch duty is missing from the local record. Ruth Nwosu, the working night steward, holds the physical key. Under an attended escort procedure, Alma enters observation four at 10:56, the route closes at 10:57:41 after 1 minute 41 seconds, and Sanaa makes Jonas record exactly what remains unverified. A separate municipal office order suspends Jonas’s independent gate use pending local duty review; it is not a second Lattice notice. Ruth’s duty is established here as ending at the 11:00 morning-watch handover. No civic term is mended.

### Chapter 0003 — The Clause in the Wall

Oren Pell closes the bridge under a valid public-safety order but invokes the separately inspected Assembly Emergency Service Test for one ambulance lane. Jonas completes the physical relay reset; afterward, the limited Fault-reader view shows the obsolete empty-crossing term linked to the ambulance conflict, and he names the fault class for the first time — a seam. Sensory bleed forces him to anchor on Nora Bell and the plate. The 90-minute lane test closes successfully but incompletely, and a separate unlisted shuttle arrives.

### Chapter 0004 — The Unlisted Stop

The green shuttle has no transit record, but it carries a child with a hand-marked token, courier Pia Santos, Alma Reyes after a worsening return journey, and an older driver who remains with the vehicle. Jonas refuses to treat the route as a rogue bridge effect or impound the vehicle while the patient deteriorates. A clinic clerk dispositions the child and patient while Pia remains with the vehicle; Jonas signs only a vehicle safety check, and the driver retains custody. The patient returns toward the old-canal shelter, where the door asks for a post-arrival duty.

### Chapter 0005 — The Price of a Door

Selah Mora refuses Jonas’s convenient offer to put his own name on an undefined shelter claim. Rafi, Mina Okafor, and Selah instead name a bounded escort: room, medication, follow-up, and closing time. Alma returns to the shelter, the route closes after a complete handoff, and Jonas recognizes the temptation to turn responsibility into control. A valid dawn closure order then forces the shelter’s residents to seek another address.

### Chapter 0006 — The Registrar’s Rule

Oren’s order is valid: the old building’s wall, canopy, and water supply are unsafe. Jonas refuses to take an inspection he is not qualified for and helps Selah move residents, possessions, and keys to Bellow Market under Mara Holt’s care. Alma and her medication reach the receiving shelter, the building is sealed, and Tomas rejects Jonas’s urge to sign a blank personal guarantee. Oren schedules the separate pump inspection.

### Chapter 0007 — No One Signed the Map

Emeka Rusk demonstrates that a real shared-maintenance duty survives without a current municipal term. Jonas separates the physical leak from the missing term but cannot write one into the other. Under pressure from an official headcount request, he refuses to trade resident identities for a route record; Vann resolves the emergency tank by metered consumption instead, so the refusal costs nothing. The pump remains closed, bottled water and a clinic tank are arranged, and management orders the bridge incident misclassified as hardware-only.

### Chapter 0008 — The Man Who Filed a Fault

Jonas identifies the I. Vey seal as part of the original Continuity Project maintenance series and finds a partial lowland service code matching hardware in Evan’s family file. Selah keeps the complete route and identities in a sealed local log; the NDC receives only a redacted custody reference with consent, purpose, and expiry limits. Tomas warns against a continuity waiver. Jonas files an unresolved permission conflict, leaving his appointment pending.

### Chapter 0009 — The First Night Shift

A clinic/shelter hinge makes the urgent patient route and Omar Sadiq’s nonurgent shelter return compete. The local review leaves Jonas’s gate-use suspension active but allows attended maintenance only after Ruth’s named watch initiates the route, and only while that watch stays in place. Jonas refuses to create a cross-node term; the urgent transfer crosses, while Omar waits exactly 43 minutes and then completes by the street route. The shelter route itself opened at 10:16 and closed unused at 10:19 because the vehicle had already been committed. Sanaa makes Jonas disclose the cost, earning limited trust without making her his ally. Tomas then requires every witness log and a visible responsibility bond before any shared-node civic edit.

### Chapter 0010 — The City Needs a Name

Under the west-lane Assembly test, a real ambulance, clinic admission, and Bellow Market shelter-to-clinic handoff run within a 90-minute attended window from 10:00 to 11:30. Ruth Nwosu’s duty ends at 11:00 and no successor exists to take the key, because the local record never carried her as a holder, so she stays to the expiry as a named witness, unpaid, while Mina keeps the paper and corridor from 11:00 and Dr. Sethi’s day team still comes on at noon. At the 9:58 round Selah reads the scheduled clinic run back to Jonas and names that it crosses the failed south span; he prices the taxi alternative at 38 minutes, writes the risk onto the scope, but cannot re-book her local claim and will not spend the shelter’s water money, so he leaves the run in place. When the physically stopped side service gate blocks it at 10:17, he makes Nina record the delay as three causes — the permission conflict, the stopped gate, and his own scope. Unsafe side-hinge pressure leads to formal isolation at 10:31 under Oren’s order. The shelter vehicle and handoff leg end at 10:55, while the formal shelter authorization closes at 11:30 on the test’s expiry rather than the patient’s condition. Jonas records the unresolved conflict instead of accepting a hardware-only report. The Old Ward board reveals an empty central root holder field with a Reclamation Season review line. Tomas blocks the next shared-node attempt until Jonas faces a visible responsibility bond.

## Rolling Handoff

The opening batch ends at Stage 1 with no active supernatural seam mark or personal claim. The bridge, clinic, and shelter routes were attended and closed; the old shelter and pump remain closed for physical reasons. Chapters 11–20 begin with Tomas’s bond and the first bounded west-clinic civic repair, then begin the separate Chapters 19–20 Lowglass route work.

## Volume 01 — Chapters 11–20

### Chapter 0011 — The Bond

Tomas Renn puts a seven-clause responsibility bond on a chair in the River Stacks shop and makes Jonas read it aloud, with the Chapter 6 transport record beside it so the difference is visible: the transport record moves a stretcher, the bond lets him write a rule. Jonas first offers a broad guarantee (“any person who requires it… until the underlying fault is resolved”). Bea Osunde asks who decides who requires it and what happens at six o’clock when the watch has gone home. On a decommissioned bench plate the broad promise returns **A promise with no end is not a term. / No bearer named. No expiry. / Nothing to hold**, and nothing happens. Jonas drafts the bond himself, adds clause seven — that it cannot touch his civil-service appointment — and Ruth Nwosu arrives for an hour paid by the shop’s dispute fund, writes her own refusal condition in the margin in block capitals, and signs as witness. The bond is posted on the shop’s yellow board, named to the west clinic care entrance, and accepted rather than approved.

### Chapter 0012 — The Door’s Terms

Ruth initiates the attendance in writing at 21:20, satisfying the narrowed gate-use suspension. The plate carries the engraved **NO UNATTENDED ACCESS — WEST CLINIC CARE ENTRANCE** and a pencilled window, 23:00–06:00, that is six hours short of her real 22:00–11:00 watch. Jonas maps the six parts of a permission on a form and finds four real, one questionable, and the **precedence** box empty. Sanaa lists three clinical needs; Ruth insists urgent only; Mina insists on a definition of verification; Selah Mora, by telephone at 23:38, consents to the return-of-care limb alone, 22:00–06:00, and refuses everything else. Ruth tapes the emergency doors and times a trolley to the ward turn — eleven minutes, blocking the emergency approach — which converts urgency from a judgment into a measurement. A dry run of a real non-urgent claim fails and the plate returns **Scope unclear. Precedence not stated. / Which promise answers is not written down.** The term is written on the back of a shift sheet, signed by all four, and the sixth line — *if no bearer is on duty, the route is held by the issuer* — is the only one the plate will take.

### Chapter 0013 — The First Mended Term

Ruth wakes Jonas at 04:50 and has written a bearer-initiated maintenance request by 05:42. With Ruth on the probe and Sanaa witnessing, Jonas restores **one field** — the precedence — and nearly loses the edit; Ruth, not Mina, calls the stop. The plate prints a narrow strip instead of a verdict, and the strip carries a line nobody discussed: **Window on record: 23:00–06:00. Window declared by the holder: 22:00–11:00**, and a sixth line, **IF NO HOLDER IS ON DUTY: the route is held by the issuer. ISSUER FALLBACK — MERCER, J. AVAILABILITY: acknowledged per shift.** Ruth reads the issuer fallback aloud and calls it a man with a door. Two real transfers succeed at 07:04 and 08:15; the plate correctly refuses a laundry trolley. Ruth hands over at 11:00 without staying a minute, and reminds Jonas that her hours are still wrong.

### Chapter 0014 — The Useful Mistake

The gate works and the crews begin using it; Jonas says yes to the service lift because the schedule does not say no, and checks. Sanaa comes down the ramp and tells him he has built a gate that no longer needs anybody. Tomas refuses a continuity rider that would reclassify the door as equipment, and reports six people who cannot honestly hold it. This time Jonas changes a decision: he moves the Bellow Market clinic run off the isolated south span onto the clinic vehicle and pays forty-one minutes out of the department budget, logged beside the earlier delay. At 21:09 he acknowledges two one-hour gaps in the night window from his kitchen. At 02:40 the next morning Ezra Mbeki moves a fall-risk patient alone because the plate believes the night watch is on duty; **Marta Sowle, ninety-one, goes down in the corridor and is found at 02:53 by a cleaner, eleven minutes later**, and the safeguarding form records the record’s own contribution. Ezra tells Jonas the acknowledgement helped nobody, because the middle of the night was never the problem. Jonas drops a screwdriver, is sent home at 12:50, says “nothing needs me until five,” and sleeps through a transfer arriving at 15:22.

### Chapter 0015 — The Price of an Open Door

**Lorna Vessey, sixty-one**, arrives with a verified claim, an assigned bed, a named receiving nurse and a 15:40 cardiac slot. The plate says **HOLDER NOT NAMED. GATE CLOSED.** Sanaa refuses to call the case an emergency to save the slot, and says why. The slot is released at 15:41; the next available date is the fourteenth. At 15:58 Jonas acknowledges the day shift and opens the door at 16:03 in knowing breach of his suspension, with Mina writing the line in the exact words he gives her. He states three things out loud: the gate worked for him and nobody else; the same page also let a patient be moved alone; and the people who can stop the next use are Ruth, Sanaa and Mina. Sanaa certifies the hardware and writes on the same form that she does not certify the permission. Nerys Vessey says her mother will be told there was a door and it was open and nobody was standing behind it, and insists her mother’s name stays off the public board while the slot stays on it.

### Chapter 0016 — What the Claim Requires

Gunnar Halvorsen, the west site’s service manager, offers a hospital continuity waiver that would have opened the ramp and would let *the issuer or a designated municipal officer* hold any gap. Jonas refuses it in nine words — a category cannot be told no — and has the refusal and the offer minuted. **Tomas names four prerequisites for a real day watch — a payroll line, a paid training afternoon, a clinic-side naming authority, and a written rule for when two people on one rota both refuse — and says the fourth is the question that will kill the plan in a fortnight. It is never answered in this batch.** Ruth refuses to have the night watch erased so the schedule can look clean, and Sanaa produces the patient notice, including the 02:40 event, which the ninety-one-year-old patient wants to read before it goes up. In the records room, Nina surfaces a registrar’s letter asking the department to classify Jonas’s two solo acknowledgements. Nell Ardent arrives as the Vessey family’s lawyer, gives no remedy and one free piece of advice, and refuses to write that the repair was sound. Bea Osunde produces the fourteen-day private rota Jonas wrote and keeps it. He decides to renegotiate the schedule and accept the debt.

### Chapter 0017 — The Existing Bearers

Six people define *available* and none of them agrees. The roster is written: Bram Osei 06:00–11:00, Ezra Mbeki 13:30–22:00, Ruth Nwosu 22:00–11:00, Selah Mora for the return-of-care limb 22:00–06:00 with the line **retainer requested in writing of Saint Orra west: not yet agreed** — she makes Mina read that line aloud and says the words stay until the hospital writes the cheque — and a named, published, unfilled gap from **11:00 to 13:30**. Mina declines the gap because she cannot hold the desk and the gate. The gap is then tested on the page and costs something: an eighty-eight-year-old's non-urgent dressing claim is refused at 12:05 with *holder not named, route closed* and admitted at 12:50 under a written clinical declaration, and both lines are logged. Ruth reads the carried cost on the board and signs the roster before she leaves. Tomas leaves his stop-work instruction and does not ask a second time about his fourth prerequisite — what happens when two people on one rota both refuse — which the roster answers for the worse. Ezra comes on at ten past one, reads the term aloud alone as the page requires, and asks whether the sixth line is still true. Marta Sowle tells Jonas to take the issuer off the door. At 14:00 Jonas makes a **second, separate edit** at the same plate, with Ezra on the probe: the issuer is removed from the fallback and a declared emergency reduction written in by the on-call clinician. The plate prints **Term unchanged. Issuer unchanged. Claim unchanged.** The seam mark extends to below the elbow. At 16:10 the Reclamation Season series 6 maintenance request arrives.

### Chapter 0018 — The First Bearing Trail

Bea opens drawer four. The series 6 chain runs from a River Stacks cabinet through the Saint Orra bridge to the clinic gate and on to **6-L/14 and 6-L/19, “Canal Lowline,” address not indexed**. The code LO/6-L/14 is the Chapter 8 lowland code and matches Evan’s family file; Jonas stops himself from reading the past backwards and says so. Four records, four blanks: the library street index, the water authority’s standpipe map, a school catchment office that needs a reference he cannot obtain. He tells Selah he thought about opening the sealed log and did not; she refuses anyway and names the Weir, the stand, and Pia Santos. The Old Ward board carries the review notice four inches above the gate’s own log.

### Chapter 0019 — Another Map

Pia sets four conditions on the towpath: no names, no photographs, no promises without local agreement, no use of the sealed log, and he leaves the tool roll at the shop. He meets Aurrie Nkemdirim, Margo Doré and Bo Trimble, learns that the Weir stand runs twenty-two minutes a day against a rota of forty because the lowland main’s silt trap packs every surge, and finds a civic plate on the loft wall with its lower face scraped, carrying **LOWLAND WARDS — WATCH ESTABLISHED FOR THE FLOOD YEARS / ANY HOUSE MAY CALL. THE WATCH ANSWERS.** Nobody knows who made it or who scraped it. He asks about a child’s brass token and does not ask twice. At the top of the Weir they find a wet patch on the inner face of the bank the size of a doorway, and Bo says nothing at all.

### Chapter 0020 — The Flood-Gate Choice

Jonas states only what he has seen from the path and refuses to be quoted for anything else. Three positions are argued and none is chosen for him: apply and be counted, take a county pump for one tide, or carry on unlisted. He prices the work — pumping, four days inside the tide table, six people who will get hurt — and names the three things any future term would need, then refuses to be any of them. He and Bo pack the sill with fresh compound, which buys a season and not a surge; he agrees to bring a coupling and a gasket on Thursday and nothing else. Bo explains that the loft’s own plate was taken down and dropped in the canal because it kept asking who was on duty at two in the morning, and tells Jonas not to make it into a story. On the towpath Pia says the number came out of his own drawer four days after he fixed the door, and that she does not know what to do with him.

## Rolling Handoff 2

The batch ends at Stage 2 with a seam mark to the elbow, a numb right hand, an unpaid maintenance claim, a public issuer line, a suspended technician’s open question with the registrar, and a released cardiac slot that no amendment has touched. The series 6 chain has produced the first claim-bearing trail toward the Weir and has explained nothing. Chapters 21–30 begin in Pia’s Lowglass service work and the flood barrier, with the clinic gate running on a local renewable term in Jonas’s absence and one published gap nobody has closed.

## Volume 01 — Chapters 21–30

### Chapter 0021 — Thursday, After Six

Jonas reads the Old Ward board at ten past five on Thursday the thirteenth and finds four gate-log lines he did not write: Bram’s 06:00 tick, the 11:00 closure, a 12:41 emergency opening declared by the on-call clinician and returned at 13:02, and Ezra’s 13:30 tick. The term expires 06:00 Wednesday the nineteenth. Bea Osunde sends him to the Weir with a canvas bag — coupling, gasket, PTFE tape and a length of galv pipe he cut himself — refuses him the tool roll, and warns him about adding a name to a rota to solve a Tuesday. On the Weir he cuts back 410 millimetres to Bo Trimble’s specification; **Wesley Kpomah tightens the joint because the wrench belongs to the tool rota and Jonas does not.** The coupling holds. Aurrie signs the specification because the person who did the work is not on the rota. Jonas then tries to have Bo put on the tool rota and is refused: *the only protection he has and I am not spending it.* The county pump-and-stone form is put on the table with four boxes and one name left to fill. Nina Calder telephones to say the barrier is now an open municipal asset defect on the series 6 chain, estimated £4,200–£6,800, asset owner blank, reporting officer Jonas, and that the registrar’s answer is due at noon tomorrow, in a room.

### Chapter 0022 — The Registrar’s Answer

In the records room: Nina Calder refuses to draft, Royden Achebe offers a version that costs the city nothing and withdraws it by name at 10:09 when Oren Pell points out that a department cannot be the holder of a plate; Tomas Renn insists the answer goes public; Sanaa Iqbal brings the clinical annex. The filed answer splits the two acknowledgements: **21:09 is a breach; 15:58 is ratified as an emergency opening and the department accepts the claim.** Consequences: Jonas stays issuer, the maintenance claim is neither discharged nor transferred, he is removed from all gate-initiation at 6-41 and from cabinet 6-R-14, the lowland nodes’ history “cannot be produced” with a physical inspection proposed, and his appointment review is deferred. Oren warns that an inspection requires a location, and a location in a form becomes an address. Back in the records room, consequence three’s form has a box that wants six words, and at 12:41 Jonas writes **6-L/14 — NON-CIVIC PLANT. 6-L/19 — NON-CIVIC PLANT**, true, and the worst thing he does this month. On the board, the term was **renewed at 22:00 on the thirteenth** with Ruth Nwosu’s written objection about her wrong hours in the box beneath it.

### Chapter 0023 — The Tide Table

Saturday, 05:05 ebb. Jonas and Bo go down the chamber on a rope. Ordinary inspection only: the sill is rotten through the depth in the middle third; the pintle is solid; and there are **two sets of bolt holes**, so the current pintle was fitted into holes that do not match the frame. The chamber wall carries a silted civic plate — **6-L/14, LOWLAND WARDS, FLAP CONTROL, LOWLAND DIP, HOLDER: —** — whose one legible condition line reads **IF THE FLAP IS NOT SHUT, THE BANK IS NOT A WALL.** Jonas tells the room in full that his own filed answer has put a number on their street. Margo reads the tide table: floods on the 8th, 15th, 22nd, 29th and 6th; springs 12th–22nd; usable window the morning ebb, 05:05–07:25. Otie names the hazard: two hours of ebb a day for four days is nine hours and ten minutes of work, and “there are two kinds of person at three in the morning.” The job is priced: crew £3,600, pump £1,900, stone £340, skip £410, materials £280, mason £600 = **£7,130**; they have £913. Bo names where the hurt is: the bank. Margo catches that Aurrie has just agreed to four neap mornings that are in fact spring mornings. Bo then proposes asking the *city* for a pump, because the department’s own proposed inspection will hire one — and the price of the pump is that somebody writes a location in a box. Aurrie pins three positions on the loft wall with a name against each and the line **THE BANK — no name yet.**

### Chapter 0024 — What the Count Is For

Sunday. **Hector Mbatha produces a 32-year door count** he has kept in a pocket diary: 61 in April 1994, 59 last April, recounted every April in eleven minutes. Aurrie admits she has wanted a district since 1994 and that she never knew he had the number. Margo produces the second number: the stand’s board says **SLOTS: 40**, and 40 is *minutes of draw a day*, not households — a water authority man put 40 into *households served* in a Saltmouth report nine years ago and it is still there. Otie’s rule: a form does not know what it asks, it only knows the shape; the leash is the *name under the number*, not the number. **Pia refuses to be a spokesperson** and refuses Jonas the room’s consent. Jonas writes a location description for the inspection that is true and unusable and reads it aloud; Aurrie makes him strike *no address* and write **NO MADE-UP ROAD. ACCESS BY FOOTPATH ONLY, ON FOOT.** Jonas states in front of them that he is not on their side and that the reason the form exists is that he opened a drawer. Mina tells him the Sunday day-porter box has been empty three Sundays running and nobody read it because nothing happened.

### Chapter 0025 — A Term at the Water Point

The stand pillar carries a second unindexed plate: **6-L/19, WEIR STAND, DRAWING POINT, HOLDER: —** with a legible duty and no precedence. Anchored on it with Bo as witness, Jonas reads a seam: a rota claim and a mains premises claim with no order between them. He proposes mending one field — precedence. **Tomas Renn telephones and stops the work**, because the bond is named to the west-clinic care entrance and Jonas is not on a rota, a clinic-side naming authority, or anything that authorises a second hinge. Tomas’s condition: bring Bea, and do not put his own name in the issuer line. Bea drives ninety minutes, stops the work a second time over the *holder field* (“you have written a name in the holder line… a rota stops meaning *these people, today, and they can put it down*”), and the fix is that the holder is **the rota**, not a person. A plate will not take a role without a name, so Margo Doré consents to **ISSUER: THE HOLDER ON THE WEIR STAND ROTA, KEEPER M. DORÉ.** Jonas performs the bounded edit. The plate prints precedence, refusal rights, an unpaid claim, a 14-day term or completion of the barrier work whichever is sooner, and renewal only by the holder on the rota reading it aloud to the next holder. **Wesley Kpomah refuses his Wednesday slot in writing on the board**; the stand runs 12 minutes instead of 22 and the 10 minutes are not made up. Jonas completes a civic repair for the first time without being its issuer.

### Chapter 0026 — The Terms of a Pump

Monday night in the loft kitchen, nine o'clock until nearly four. The arithmetic: £7,130 all-in, £4,630 without pump and mason, £1,030 without the marine crew, £913 in hand, a £117 gap. **Margo Doré refuses box four in writing** — *a person who cannot be reached at 03:00 must not sign for machinery* — and says she would do it badly. **Hector Mbatha refuses** because a name in that box gives the street an owner, and an owner is the one who is on the bank at three in the morning, and he will not be that and neither should anybody else. **Aurrie Nkemdirim is the only one who could sign and refuses anyway**, because the day her name is in that box the street has an owner and the *owner* is responsible at three in the morning instead of the street, and belonging to nobody is the only civic technology this street has had since before she came to it. Bo removes the skip from the price because an unpumped chamber is not cut. **Perdita Sow**, a borough road sweeper who has swept that road on Sundays for eleven unpaid years, takes a seat — for the habit, not for bravery. Jonas offers to go on the bank and is refused by five people separately. Margo reads out the bottom line of the paper, **THE BANK — anyone who will take it, and there is no name on that line because there is no name yet**, and then Jonas, told to leave the rest blank and put a ring round the blank on purpose, writes: **THE 22ND, AT 03:00, ON A BANK. NO NAME.** He also corrects the wall paper's hour — 04:00 is the muster, not the working time, and the water is out at 05:05. Jonas confesses, unasked, that he was the man in the van in the coat in 2019 who told Bo the gasket was the wrong size, and that Hector had told him the day before, at the kitchen table, that the man had his face and he had said nothing.

### Chapter 0027 — What Is Under the Sill

Friday the twenty-first, 04:10, the fourth and last working morning. Jonas corrects Margo on the tide table: in a neap the low is 1.5 m and the sill top is 1.10 m below datum, so a neap chamber never clears the beam; only a spring low empties it. Margo accepts it in one paragraph and says she has been wrong about the ebb for 29 years. The 05:05 ebb empties the chamber to eight hundred millimetres of freeboard. The flap is found to have been shut **by hand on the ebb for 29 years** — the operating error behind the boil. The rotten middle third comes out in two hours and forty minutes. Under the frame: a **lead service seal stamped 6-L/14 · SERVICE · LOWLAND WORKS · XI·17 · S&D IW**, eleven years old. Under that, paint on the concrete — **DO NOT CLOSE ON THE EBB. CLOSE ON THE FLOOD. 6-L/14 — LOWLANDS — 8·III** — and on the sound cheek of the beam a cold-chisel **E·M**. Jonas wants it to be Evan; it cannot be, because the seal is eleven years old and Evan died eighteen years ago and never worked for a district ironworks. **Bo refuses to carry the letters up the path for him** and says he has a date on it. The new ash beam (£140, of which £39 was Aurrie’s own) and the new pintle go in; the flap closes itself on the 02:10 flood. The barrier holds for four hours and ten minutes, will not hold a surge, and has no name on it for the next flood. The seal and the beam go into an oilcloth envelope in a tin. **Hector asks for the tin and refuses it** — *a thing that is held is a thing that is yours.* **Ottie Beck takes it**, into her spares tin, unlabelled. Nobody may tell the county or the department it exists.

### Chapter 0028 — A Separate Kind of Pressure

Saturday the twenty-second. At 01:00 on Friday the trap packed and **Delphine Ofori**, holder in the first rota slot, closed the stand herself and crossed the slot. At 02:30 Ottie needed water for a four-year-old with a fever and could not get it, because the mended precedence required *the holder* and the holder was in bed. Bo: *you wrote a rule that needs a person.* At 02:10 the flood comes over the path; the flap shuts itself; Perdita is alone on the bank and rings the loft to say two words. The barrier holds. **Ottie Beck pins THE LOWLAND DIP — WHAT WAS DONE AND WHAT IT COST on the Old Ward public board** — the first thing from that street on a civic board — refusing a list of houses and refusing any number the city did not put on itself. At 14:10 **Royden Achebe’s classification of 6-L/14 and 6-L/19 as non-civic plant** is lodged into the series 6 file, and it is the six words Jonas wrote himself on the fourteenth. Oren Pell calls it wrong and gives Jonas the only move that does not put his name on a lie: a **refusal to classify**, on the ground that the local custodians were asked for a statement of function and declined. **Pia Santos refuses to help him withdraw it** and states the demand that the batch ends on — not a count, not a survey, not a statement: *who is the other end of it.*

### Chapter 0029 — The Second Map

Monday the twenty-fourth. Bea refuses Jonas the wall cabinet: consequence two bars him from 6-R-14 and there is no review of the review yet, so the series 6 response will be written without the cards. She tells him he has put three things under his own name and there are now eleven on the Weir under somebody else’s, and that he has stopped being the answer there without noticing. At the Old Ward, a service diagram has **one filled circle and one open square** four millimetres apart, in a place with no name — the first mark he has ever put on the city’s map, from a mended term about a tap. Beside it, clipped to the frame, is **Hector’s pencil map: THE WEIR. 59 DOORS. DRAWN BY H. MBATHA, 24 MARCH.** At 12:02 a stretcher comes up the Saint Orra ramp; the plate says HOLDER NOT NAMED; Mina telephones the on-call clinician; **Sanaa does not answer for nine minutes because she is in a resuscitation room** — the day she named. Jonas cannot open the door and does not. **Bram has refused Thursday** and the board now says 06:00 NO HOLDER on a weekday morning, because the roster does not say who covers a refusal. Gunnar Halvorsen signs a **rostering exception correcting Ruth’s hours to 22:00–11:00 from the fifteenth** and says it does nothing for nineteen years. Ruth, asked properly for the first time, refuses the number: *a hundred and eighteen thousand pounds would become the argument, not me.* Selah Mora rings Halvorsen and makes him read the retainer line back; he commits to the **ninth of next month** and says a diary entry is not a cheque. At the shop, **Tomas Renn asks his fourth question in person, once**, and tells Jonas not to answer it.

### Chapter 0030 — The Price of an Unlisted Service

Tuesday the twenty-fifth into Wednesday. Jonas drafts the series 6 response; Nina Calder forces the honest reason into it; Ruth corrects *term expiring* to the fact that the term was read out on the twenty-fourth and runs to 06:00 on the thirtieth, and tells him to read every document to somebody outside the department. Margo Doré reads the draft back and finds the real defect: *there is no “is” in it — you have written a true document about a thing that is not there.* Then she shows him the stand: since Monday the tap runs by itself and the plate **no longer asks her anything**, because the term read **14 days from the reading OR the completion of the barrier work, whichever is sooner**, and the barrier work finished at 13:30 on Friday the twenty-first. Nobody noticed for five days. When the rota does not answer, the mains wins, and the practical consequence of *non-civic plant* is not a line on a map; it is a tap. Margo refuses to be a category and refuses again. Bo says withdraw the classification and let the review ask. Jonas names the four bodies that could be served and says the decision is not his and not theirs. The Old Ward board carries two blank holder fields a hundred yards apart: **CENTRAL ROOT — HOLDER: —** and **6-L/19 — LOCAL TERM ISSUED 17 MARCH. HOLDER: ROTA. EXPIRED 21 MARCH, 13:30.** Bea confirms the term can only be renewed by the rota reading it aloud to the rota and tells him to go and see the service manager. Halvorsen will spend one of forty minutes of *matters arising* on the ninth. At the clinic, at 22:15, the term is read aloud in the middle of the corridor and Ruth says *you have not read them*, and he does.

### Chapter 0031 — The First Offer

Thursday the twenty-seventh of March. A CivicCore van in the River Stacks yard fixes a barrier gate in forty minutes that the department has had for three days, and the call-out is charged whether or not the fault is found. **Ceri Vaughan**, CivicCore's continuity counsel, makes a four-part offer in a room over a tool-hire place on Marsh Lane: a permanent appointment at forty-four thousand, root-level notice access, **the continuity of every term Jonas has ever issued with no expiry**, and in exchange the **blanket continuity waiver** — no local term, holder, refusal or standing practice is incompatible with continuity maintenance, and *the refusal of any person, rota or institution does not suspend, delay or condition continuity maintenance.* A fifth part, the arrears and the return-of-care retainer paid retrospectively into the west site's own participation fund, is the most tempting thing in the room. Vaughan refuses to say Ruth's number aloud because it is not written down. She states plainly what the offer would do to a street with no address: the first person who ever does anything for it would be a company, and the company does not have neighbours. **Jonas counts what it would cost him in people, in two columns, in his own pad, and closes it.** He neither signs nor refuses. At dusk the Weir stand is found closed, with a pencil time on the board and nine houses with no water, and the offer is open.

### Chapter 0032 — What Rook Can Show

Friday the twenty-eighth. Nina Calder files the series 6 response. **Alden Rook** demonstrates, and wins. Morrow Fields Plot 6: a pump the department left forty-seven days and CivicCore attended in four hours forty, for a thirty-pound part sitting in a 2011 addendum nobody owns. Tarn Street Primary: a **continuous** west-gate term that emptied on 22 November when a deputy head moved to Kestrel Hill and did not lapse — it simply had nobody in it — so the gate became a latch for eighty-two days and a thousand children a week went through it by hand; CivicCore did not fix it, it got the school to name a porter. **Morah Bonsu** asks Jonas what a school with no roll and no address would do, and answers: a head teacher standing at her own gate is not a permission, it is a woman holding a door. Gantry Ward's service floor: a chalk board, 240 customers, 41 hinges open, nine of them not Rook's staff, and **Wren Court** in Salter's Yard — thirty-one rooms, no civic address, a named steward, better service than the west clinic, and a clause nine that disconnects for non payment. Jonas invents the only good counter-argument he has — a claim survives the responder, a price stops at the invoice — and Rook's answer is *then name the claim.* Jonas **agrees with Rook aloud**, and says it will make the rest of his week much harder. On the pavement Rook predicts the review exactly: a panel cannot act on the absence of a finding, and the cheapest way to clear an open item is to accept the reporting officer.

### Chapter 0033 — The Waiver, Refused

Saturday the twenty-ninth, 09:30, a briefing room, six people, one page in a document tray. **Royden Achebe dismantles Jonas's objection before he makes it**: no clause in the document stops a rota keeping its own door, and Jonas says so, and admits he has been trying to find one since eleven o'clock the previous night and that every reason he has got is about himself. **Sanaa Iqbal is telephoned from a corridor with four hundred patients** and makes the best argument in the city for the page — it is the clause that would have stopped her being nine minutes late — and then says she does not want it, because the day the door opens for anybody who claims it she stops being the person who decides, and asks for that to be minuted. **Bea Osunde**, asked, says she is not going to help him, and then says the thing that is not on his side: he is the only man in the city anybody can point at and say *you were here when it started.* **Jonas refuses, for two new reasons** — he cannot tell whether he is signing as a man who believes it or a man who needs the cabinet, the appointment and the arrears, and the form has no box for that; and the page works by naming whoever is closest, and the nearest responsible person on the lowland chain is him. Nina Calder minutes all three parts and it goes on the public board. **The cost:** appointment declined, grade three and probationary; the maintenance claim assigned in writing to the nearest responsible maintenance route; two CivicCore crews off the Saint Orra queue; the participation fund withdrawn. Margo Doré puts the telephone down on him. At 21:50 the term is renewed at 22:00 to **06:00 on 4 April** and Ruth Nwosu says she does not know the answer to why she reads five lines to a man every week.

### Chapter 0034 — A Separate Closure

Sunday the thirtieth, a basement, nine steps down. **Brine Wharf, Cooper's Reach**: eleven flats, a Thursday clinic of forty-one patients, and in the basement **Lowland Refuge Store 1, series 6 plate 6-L/21**, the only registered refuge capacity for that bank — foam, two thousand four hundred sandbags, a pump, a generator, blankets — with an **empty holder field, three painted initials from 1988, and eleven centimetres of lettering chiselled off.** The plate's only line: **IF THIS HOLDER IS EMPTY, THIS STORE IS NOT A STORE.** Oren Pell has come to tell Jonas, in person, that emptying it puts a third unclaimed burden on the nearest responsible maintenance route on the series 6 chain, which is him. The January survey found thirty-four millimetres of drop and a monitoring pin; the pin is gone. **Jonas measures it himself: fifty-four millimetres, three worse than the registrar's figure, in the corner the escape route discharges into — and says so, and makes the closure more certain rather than less.** The renewed order is made at 17:40, served at 17:52, effective at 18:00, on the figure measured on the twenty-second, because a person may not be moved out on a measurement taken the day the order is made. Eleven flats go to a bought-out hostel for fourteen nights, the last of them the night of 12 April, and from the thirteenth nobody has paid for anything. **Pia Santos refuses alignment with the Weir in the yard**, and Yasmin Rohan refuses it again inside. The store goes to a lock-up on Marsh Lane with a key on a seven-to-seven clock.

### Chapter 0035 — The Appeal and the Price

Monday the thirty-first, a laundry room. **Nell Ardent reads the appeal form out twice** and prices the appeal: a hearing on 23 April, twenty-three days' notice against a fourteen-day minimum, representation required, **£340 deposit, £2,000 continuation bond payable precisely because they will probably lose**, and the fourth requirement — state in your own words what the safety position would be if the building stayed open. The building is shut. The store is behind a seven-to-seven clock. The appeal does not turn on fairness or on the building being safe; it turns on an arithmetic the panel cannot hear. **The Weir cannot be a party**, because a party is a person or an institution with a name on a register, and there is no address, no classification that survives the review, and no unexpired term — and **Oren Pell forbids the Weir's evidence from the appeal** and says why, which costs them their best argument. **CivicCore's written offer** to hold the store and stand the clinic for eleven weeks is declined by Yasmin Rohan in one sentence: *in the fourth year of this we would be a client.* **Jonas refuses to bring the loft's paper into the room**, for the reason that a document and an appeal in one bundle become two examples of the same problem and the answer to both is a name. **Oren Pell states the last shape: eleven flats, forty-one patients, two thousand four hundred sandbags, and one name.** Yasmin Rohan writes one name in the fourth line of the form and reads it out to nobody.

### Chapter 0036 — The Assembly Sits

**Tuesday the first of April**, two rooms, one day, in a public gallery of forty-one. The **series 6 review** takes Jonas's three options and none of them: he will not maintain the recommendation, will not withdraw it, and **refuses to read the legible condition line on the 6-L/14 plate into a public record**, on the ground that a minute is a list and the only way a street ever gets an address is if somebody living there is made findable in order to produce one. It is minuted as a decision, not an oversight, and the line goes into the file unread. Oren Pell objects on filing grounds only: a node with a lapsed holder is not a node with no holder. The decision is split: **6-L/14 accepted as plant, no civic claim attaching to the chamber or the bank; 6-L/19 not accepted, in scope pending an inspection that is not scheduled because there is no location of record.** In the afternoon the **Assembly delays the continuity contract** to a committee report on 2 June, receives the loft's paper as a document over Jonas's objection — **no door number on it, no list of houses, and nobody named**, as Sore certifies after reading it twice, with Jonas's only intervention being to point out that it does not name him and her answer that this is the last thing left in the city that is not. It then **loses Iven Sore's motion on the standing orders twice** — this House may not require a person to accept a duty, and the motion needs a service whose existence was not established that morning — and ratifies the Brine Wharf order. Then it creates an **interim lowland maintenance post** and names the reporting officer for plate 6-41. **Jonas declines in the room.** Royden Achebe takes it in his own name, on the ground that a man can hold a plate and a department cannot, having withdrawn a draft on 14 March in the registrar's answer for exactly the opposite reason. Jonas tries to raise the contradiction and is ruled out of time. Afterwards **Jonas says the third thing to Bea Osunde himself**, and she tells him it is not sufficient, that he needs to be able to say it to somebody who does not care, that the real job is walking into rooms and asking people for things, that there is no instrument for it, and that she is not his conscience.

### Chapter 0037 — One Name Is Not Enough

Wednesday the second of April, in work. The department has to service a CivicCore-fitted gate with a proprietary captive nut and no catalogued part, and Jonas holds the plate square with a roofing slate and his forearm because his right hand will not close. He asks **Nora Bell**, nineteen years at the Saint Orra crossing, what happens to her — and has no answer that is not about him. She does not refuse; she asks for the week, not the ninety seconds, and gives him **Monday the fourteenth of April at half past three**, on her own ground, refuses any Thursday, and refuses his paper. Bea takes the job card out of his hand: a card with a name and a day and a time is fine, a card with a subject on it is a list, and **a hearing is the one room in this city where a list is not a list.** At Bellow Market he asks **Fola Adeyemi** — a gas account, a supplier's Thursday deadline, a landlord with four other cook-houses, a licence with her name on it, a rota with eleven people on it, and *you do not need a cook, you need somebody nobody can dismiss* — **and she refuses, in writing, in full.** At the clinic **Mina Okafor** is recruited properly: three conditions in her own hand, the third being that she withdraws on the ninth of April if there is no cheque. Two names, seven ruled lines still empty, nine lines in all, and the word *witness* written at the top of a job card on the Thursday morning of the twenty-sixth of March.

### Chapter 0038 — What Two Refusals Look Like

Tuesday 1 April at 22:00 the term is renewed, read aloud, to 06:00 on Tuesday 8 April, and for about eleven seconds the plate's HOLDER NOT NAMED is untrue. On Wednesday 2 April afternoon **Ruth Nwosu refuses her shift in writing** — four nights out of five and a back — and tells Mina to write her hours in her own hand, and **Selah Mora refuses the return-of-care limb by telephone at 16:20** because her mother has a chest and her daughter had nobody. At 22:00 on the Wednesday, with the incoming holder absent, Ezra Mbeki reads the five lines to the clerk and signs the outgoing box and the plate prints **HOLDER NOT NAMED. ROUTE CLOSED**, and he asks for the log to read *offered, refused, twice* rather than *no cover available*, and writes down that his mother is in the ward at the east site and there is nobody else to go. **The one clock: the car at 06:00 on Thursday 3 April, the declaration at 06:12, the door at 06:19, six minutes up the ramp, the baby out at 06:31.** A woman of nineteen in labour is brought up a dark ramp. Sanaa answers on the second ring, from a kitchen table in Kestrel Hill, doing discharge letters she volunteered for — **thirteen minutes against nine, and the difference is her and not the design.** Ivo Bamba holds a broken handrail in the dark with his shoulder. **Marta Sowle, ninety-one, is awake**, asks who is holding the door, and tells Mina to put *holder not named, door opened on a declaration, nineteen minutes* in the public box, and tells Jonas to keep his name off it. **Tomas Renn takes the call at ten past seven and does not let Jonas have it**: that is not an answer, that is three people and a piece of luck, and he is asking Jonas to decide now whether he will be able to say that in public in four months. On Saturday 5 April Jonas rebolts the handrail, which is not a plate and needs no witness and has never once been refused.

### Chapter 0039 — The Instance and the Fragment

**Friday the fourth of April**, twenty to six in the morning, in a market yard. Eleven traders are locked out of their own stock at a loading hatch that **opens and shuts, four seconds open, six shut**, because a collectors' self-collect term and a borough food-safety hold have no order between them and the plate is obeying both. The one civic notice is a condition and offers no solution: **WHEN TWO TERMS ANSWER TO EACH OTHER, THE HATCH IS BOTH OPEN AND SHUT.** Jonas cannot read the fault, let alone mend it, because the holder must instruct it and the only named person refuses on the telephone. **Fola Adeyemi comes down at four minutes past six in her apron, on her own, and says she is not doing it for him** — **rule eleven of the collectors' rules puts her in the holder field for the day's window and her name in the witness field beside it, a name she has carried at that hatch for twenty-two years and consented to again in the room in front of eleven people, and the holder field is the one that does not let her go home**. While the cover is off, **a brass service tag falls out of the housing**: the size of a two-pound coin, punched, torn from its twin at the crimp, stamped **C.P. SERIES 6 · LOWLAND WORKS · XI·17** and hand-inked **6-L/14 · 6-L/19 · 6-40 — SET OF THREE — RETURN ALL THREE.** It is evidence that a bridge relay and two lowland nodes were one service. It is not an answer. **Jonas gives it to her, and it goes in an apron pocket, and stays gone.** The bounded edit follows: one field, precedence only, **at a hinge he does not own, under a named holder's own instruction and consent**, issuer the market's working chef, claim unpaid. The hold lifts at 11:20, the collection happens more than seven hours outside the term's own window, and a fish business loses the following Saturday, which is the day a fish business makes its rent.

### Chapter 0040 — Nine Names, Not Yet

**Saturday the fifth of April.** Jonas writes up a **job card** he ruled himself on the Thursday morning of the twenty-sixth of March, standing up in a yard: nine lines, nine services, seven names, two blank. **Nora Bell**, Mina Okafor (conditions, dated the ninth), **Emeka Rusk** (read the five lines back twice and said he would rather be named than be the man who is not there), **Teo Vlah** (machines only, and he made Jonas write it in the line before he said yes), **Roz Idris** (not for anything a child is inside, not for anything she has been told is failing by the person who wants it fixed) and **Kofi Sarr** (a platform and never a passenger) — six. **Fola Adeyemi's refusal is written on the card in full**, because a roster that showed only consent was a roster that had been cleaned. **Lines seven and nine are empty and stay empty**: a Lowglass resident and a hospital, and Jonas has two names and has decided not to ask either — because a woman of nineteen who lives on a street this city classified as plant three days ago would be the only person on a list of nine that could be produced in a hearing — and cannot tell anybody why without making it about himself. **Tomas Renn reads the card upside down in the yard** and says the refusal written down in full is the one he is proudest of and the one in the wrong direction. At 22:00 Ruth reads the five lines, signs, and says she will do the eighth and not the fifteenth. Then Jonas goes up the towpath, is asked to wait at the bottom of the slipway, and **hears Delphine Ofori, seventy-eight, read the weir stand term aloud to Bo Trimble, seventeen** — a slot refused in writing, ten minutes not made up, and a boy who is now findable at a water point on a street with no address. The term runs to **06:00 on Saturday 19 April**; the trap is packed, the tarp is off the grille, the boil is back, and a flood is forecast for **Thursday the seventeenth**, two days before it runs out. **He does not hear the last two lines of the term; the wind takes them, and he is not going to be told them again by a woman of seventy-eight on a night when she was tired.** **Pia Santos tells him the ninth is in four days, the loft has decided the paper goes up unedited, and Aurrie will read it and will not stand up to do it** — because a woman of sixty-one standing up in a hospital boardroom is a petition and a petition gets filed, and the only power that street has is that there is nothing on any list, and one of the most powerful things any of them could do is stand up and be a person with a name and an address. At half past ten he puts a bucket under the tap. The plate asks the rota a question for the first time in fifteen days, and the answer it gets is a board, a pencil, and a woman of sixty-three reading it in the dark.

## Rolling Handoff 4

The batch ends with the contractor's bargain opened, spent and refused, and a city more entangled than it was on the twenty-sixth of March. A contract is delayed to 2 June rather than defeated. A flap gate built with a church-hall tin and six people's bodies has been certified plant, so the next flood has no institutional owner at all. A stand is in scope and cannot be inspected, because inspection needs an address and there is not one. A refuge store holding two thousand four hundred sandbags is in a lock-up whose keyholder's rota starts at seven in the morning. An appeal is lodged carrying one woman's name. An interim post Jonas refused in the room has been taken, in his own name, by the man who wrote the classification that took the street out of scope.

Jonas is Stage 2 and unchanged in capability and changed in standing: no appointment, a written unclaimed burden, a refusal he cannot unsay, a condition line he refused to put in a public record, and a decision not to ask the two people he wants most. He has six of nine witnesses, one recorded refusal, and two lines he left blank on purpose. The Weir stand term has been renewed by the rota to Saturday 19 April, at the cost of a boy of seventeen becoming findable, with a flood forecast for Thursday the seventeenth.

Chapters 41–48 complete the nine-witness coalition and close Volume 01 with the surge, the map with a blank centre, and the public title. Nothing about the Quiet Cut, the civic erasure, the watch term's origin, Evan or Iona has been explained, and a piece of net-loft paper is now a city document.

## Volume 01, Batch 0005 — Chapters 41–48 (VOLUME ENDING)

### Chapter 0041 — The Ninth Is Not a Place

**Monday 7 April into Wednesday 9 April.** Jonas goes up the towpath to fill the seventh line. **Aurrie Nkemdirim refuses him in four lines, read back and not altered**, standing in the exchange doorway: *a name on a list is not a name on a list, a name on a list is a place to send a letter*, and a document with an address becomes a door in her own wall. **Pia Santos refuses separately and separately and requires that it be written once**, so that a hearing cannot say there were two people on the seventh line. Jonas writes both on the step in the rain in his left hand and does not argue. The one notice: **THE INTAKE IS SILTED. THE POINT IS SERVING FROM A SILTED INTAKE.**

**Monday 20:50, the corridor at Saint Orra west, in front of Mina Okafor and Ruth Nwosu, Jonas asks Sanaa Iqbal to be the ninth.** **She refuses**, and gives a reason he had not prepared her with: a doctor who both declares emergencies and certifies the term that authorises them is a conflict of interest on the face of a document, and the first thing anybody in June will read is that. **Then she tells him she has been trying to get a second named clinician on that roster since 24 March — four refusals and one no from the education office — and had not told him because he would have made it a thing about a card.** He asks. She says yes, it was. She goes. At 22:00 Ruth reads the five lines and the term runs to 06:00 Wednesday 15 April, and Jonas asks her once about the fourteenth and is refused in four words.

**Wednesday 9 April, the west site board.** Aurrie Nkemdirim reads the loft's paper **sitting down throughout**, because a woman of sixty-one standing up in a hospital boardroom is a petition and a petition gets filed. Halvorsen asks two questions, both about money. The retainer minute passes at 11:20; the cheque is raised on the fourteenth. The line stays **not yet agreed**. **At 15:35 on the 9th, Mina Okafor strikes the second line out of the card in her own hand and writes under it: three conditions were set on 2 April, the third was a date, there is no cheque.** She is still the clerk. She is not the witness. Those are two facts and Jonas is not allowed to use the second one.

### Chapter 0042 — What the Night Has For

**Monday 14 April.** The Methodist hall on Carrow Lane: eleven Brine Wharf households, second night, nowhere for a third. **Nell Ardent refuses the Marsh Lane lock-up as unreachable by consent** — the keyholder is a man with a key, a timesheet and a company, and asking him is how a store becomes reachable by asking. The cliff-side lorry is a Thursday vehicle on a Thursday rota. The appeal is on the 23rd; the fourth line of the form has one name in it and it is not hers; enforcement afterwards is £95 an hour. Her only useful sentence: *the count is not free, the count is the day somebody comes with a clipboard.*

**Kofi Sarr reports the second question asked a third time**, by a man in a good coat with a laminated card, who did not ask how many people he waves through — he asked who he would telephone if the barrier end went down — and that there is a council flood forum dinner on 2 May.

**15:30, the barrier end. Nora Bell takes line one, on her own narrowed terms: I do not say who crosses it, and I want it in the writing that nobody has asked me to latch that gate once in nineteen years.** She has been offered a seat, an annual allowance and a badge on the council flood forum, **twice**, and has refused twice, and a man with a lanyard asked her the second question on the 7th. She says the hole herself: a person who latches a gate is not the holder of a gate, and a witness is not a holder, and the first thing anybody does with a record of her is ask her to write down two hundred people a day. She keeps his paper and writes her scope on it. The one notice: **A PERSON WHO LATCHES A GATE IS NOT THE HOLDER OF A GATE.** At 16:20 Dev Singh gives him the tide sheet: **THURSDAY 17TH, OVERNIGHT INTO FRIDAY. 2.9 M ABOVE SPRING HIGH AT SALTMOUTH. REVISE DOWNWARD.** Three days, and the stand term runs out two days after the water arrives.

### Chapter 0043 — The Ninety Seconds and the Week

**Wednesday 15 April, 05:05, the lowland dip.** Jonas is at the bottom of the slipway with a lamp and **nobody comes**. Margo Doré will not go because she opens the exchange at seven, and because Bo is asleep until six and has a term at the stand. And when Jonas offers to do it himself, she refuses for the reason that matters: *if you go down that bank at five past five you stop being a technician who comes to help a street and you become a claim, and a claim is a thing that does not come.* **The trap is not cleared.** The one question anybody has asked in a fortnight that was useful: *where are people who do not live here going to be.*

**Halvorsen telephones at 10:10: the cheque has not cleared and will in three or four days.** Selah Mora says she will not argue with Mina Okafor about it, and that she knows a man has been putting words in it for four weeks and she has been the one doing it.

**11:52, the corridor. The care term expired at 06:00 and was not renewed.** A woman of eighty-four is found dead in bay six; the mortuary slot is for one o'clock. **Two refusals**: Bram Osei, off at eleven, whose reason is that his authority is a key and a key is not a permission; and Ruth Nwosu, on a shift she had started at 22:00 the previous night and had been on for nearly fourteen hours of, who refused the date in advance. **The least-bad option is Ivo Bamba, twenty-nine, night orderly, on since ten o'clock the previous night, not on the rota, carrying a laundry bag**, who puts it on the floor and asks *is it the ramp*. Declaration at 11:54, door at 12:04, bier up the ramp at 12:20, the mortuary van's tail-lift shut at **12:30 — thirty-six minutes from the declaration, half an hour inside the slot** — and **the family told at ten past one instead of half past twelve: forty minutes**, and that is the single figure that goes in the public box, because **Marta Sowle sets the rule out loud before the number exists: the box counts what a family was left not knowing, and it does not count how long a door took.** Mina Okafor writes the rule in first, refuses to enter a figure she has not watched happen, and credits the rule to Marta Sowle in the box.

**The shop yard, evening. Tomas Renn's submission is written, dated, in his own hand, and has been given to Jonas to read.** Two of four stewards will not sign — Ada Kwarteng, who reads it as the shop having stopped asking, and Cuthbert Nkemelu, who thinks the last sentence is a provocation and is right. The finding: *the civic term architecture was not the failure on the third of April and was not the failure on the fifteenth, and the record should not be permitted to suggest otherwise.* **The cost is that a demand becomes a finding, a finding can only be cited, and it will be cited first by a contractor.** And then, in the rain, his own counter-finding: the architecture **was** the problem, because a renewal clause requires two people in a corridor at ten at night and cannot require anybody to be there. **The question is not answered and Jonas does not answer it.**

### Chapter 0044 — Nine Names and a Clock

**Wednesday 16 April, half past four, the River Stacks yard**, in the rain, with a piece of hardboard on a crate carrying two dates four days apart: **THURSDAY 17TH, 2.9 M** and **WEIR STAND TERM EXPIRES 06:00 SATURDAY 19TH.** The one notice: **A FLOOD IS FORECAST. NO TERM ANSWERS TO A FORECAST.**

**Bea Osunde refuses, in front of everybody, to be on the card** — a witness who works for the man mending the term is not a witness, she is a second pair of hands with a name on it. Tomas Renn is in the doorway for eleven minutes and does not come in. Mina Okafor stands at the back in her coat and watches and is nothing.

**The roll call, and anybody may refuse at the moment their name is read.** **One, Nora Bell**, narrowed. **Two, a door — Bram Osei**, who asks that his refused Thursday stays on the public board where it is. Then Emeka Rusk's telephone goes: **the overflow chamber at the Carrow Lane outfall does not drain, on Thursday night the choice is a hand wheel, the wheel is his, and the other way comes down on the market drainage, and he is telling twenty people in a yard on a Wednesday afternoon because on Friday morning it is two o'clock in the morning and nobody can be told anything.** Teo Vlah will go and look at the motor and put his name on a piece of paper and that is all he does. **Three, a market — Bisi Alade, forty-one, fish**, who takes it on the condition that the record says the mended hatch cost her a Saturday. **Four, Emeka Rusk.** **Five, Teo Vlah, machines only, said first and loudest.** **Six, a school — Roz Idris stands up before he finishes the sentence and refuses in the yard, in full.** Ibrahim Sesay, fifty-one, takes it as a key and says that opening that gate on a Thursday night without instruction is trespassing in his own building and that it goes on the card as a key. **Seven, a street — the Weir's four lines are read out, and Yolanda Fofana of Wren Court takes it only after Jonas says out loud, at her insistence, that she is not the answer to the Weir.** Three conditions, including that the fee does not stop. **Eight, Kofi Sarr**, at the gate, a platform and never a passenger, said twice. **Nine, a hospital — Dr. Sethi asks to speak before the name is read**, and takes it as a *west clinic* and not a hospital, and requires the record to say that he named the porters and has not asked them. **Eight names, five refusals in the lines they belong in, and line six a key with no holder.** Dev Singh arrives at ten past eight with a county letter saying the procedure for a silt trap is *county*, and goes home, because it is his daughter's birthday.

### Chapter 0045 — The Surge Comes on the Seventeenth

**Thursday 17 April into Friday 18 April, 06:00.** High water at Saltmouth 03:10; 910 mm of rise either side of 03:00. **02:10 the flap shuts itself on the flood, unattended, as designed, and the plate prints one line — THE FLAP IS SHUT — and 04:20 the bank goes**, taking the top two feet of the inner face out into the dip, and the dip fills to within a metre of the road in four minutes. **That was written on the loft wall in March and it is not a failure of anybody's work.**

**Emeka Rusk holds the storm overflow for an hour and ten minutes** so that eleven traders do not lose another Saturday. **Bisi Alade, standing in fifty feet of cold-store water, tells him to turn it over.** He does, alone, in the dark, at fifty-one, and told nobody until the Monday. **The Marsh Lane substation trips at 04:11 and is dark for nine hours; the cause on the fault report is surge current and the operator box is blank and stays blank.** **Ibrahim Sesay opens the school at 02:20 on his own key with no instruction, and Roz Idris, who is not on the card, tells him to shut it at 04:00**, and twenty people spend an hour in the rain on a playing field, and **Teresa Nkemelu hires two minibuses with her own money and charges the parish nothing.** **Kofi Sarr closes bay four at 02:11 and is right**, and it is also why sixty people walk four and a half miles. **Nora Bell keeps the westbound trolley signal for eleven minutes and is relieved of her post for the rest of the night.** **Perdita Sow is on the bank at 04:20 and is in the water four minutes; she is alive, with a cut thigh, a sprained wrist and a dislocated finger**, and a boy of nineteen on a towpath with a bicycle gets her out, and nobody has asked his name. The Methodist hall takes water at 04:55 and eleven households are out of it at six in the morning.

**01:40, a woman in a good coat comes up the ramp with a laminated card and a folder and does not go through the door.** **Paragraph 11 of the Saint Orra service agreement of 2019 has a second half: a node is in failure where its civic term has not been continuously in force for more than twenty-four consecutive hours.** The care term was not continuously in force for **forty hours** between 06:00 on 15 April and 22:00 on 16 April, and that is true, and Jonas says so himself. His answer is that the clause measures continuity and cannot decide what a term *is*. **At 02:20, from a car on the Salt Row embankment, Oren Pell reads paragraph 11 back to him, enters two findings in one record in one hand — a term that ends on its own terms has not failed, and a contractor's contractual definition of failure is not a civic determination of a node's state — and suspends the determination.** He also enters that the door would have been shut at ten past three and a woman of ninety-one moved in a flooding corridor on a doctor's own signature, which is worse than anything in the agreement. He says: *this is not a victory, and I gave them the witness by being in a car instead of in a room, and I have run out of ways of not doing it.* Ceri Vaughan says two true things and leaves with the only evidence of the night. Marta Sowle is moved out of bay nine at 04:04 with Ruth Nwosu in the door and Ivo Bamba holding it. **Mina Okafor writes the good line in the box and then says in the same breath that in June it will be quoted as proof it was handled, and that it is false, because nobody organised one hour of it and none of it survives Wednesday.**

### Chapter 0046 — The Bounded Handover

**Friday 18 April.** In the loft kitchen, the water has gone down 400 mm and a floor of wet people is drying in a childcare exchange. Jonas gets eighteen inches of silt out of the weir trap with a bucket, alone, in nine minutes, and puts the bucket on the step and tells nobody, and it is in no record and it bought a fortnight.

**12:20, the Saint Orra bridge, west-lane hinge**, the only hinge he is allowed to work on. The south span stays isolated; the obsolete empty-crossing term stays untouched, and Dev Singh says at the end that it is still in the file and still the reason an ambulance loses eleven minutes on a Tuesday, and that it is his, and it is not a flood, and nobody is going to pay him for it. **Issuer D. Singh, because a plate will not take a role without a name. Claim the barrier keeper's, unpaid, and expressly not the issuer's and not the mender's. Witness Nora Bell. Second bearer Yvonne Amoah, who demanded the wording *at the time*.** The lane is used only when both agree at the crossing; each may stop it; **a handover is a fresh act of both, spoken, in daylight, and the person receiving it answers for it.** **Two handovers in the log in fifty minutes, which is the proof that it is not an office.** The one notice the hinge itself prints: **A WITNESSED HANDOVER IS RECORDED AMONG THE HOLDERS. IT GRANTS NOBODY AN OFFICE.** (**Ch 46 runs over the ordinary one-notice-per-chapter ceiling in `bible/power-system.md`: three plate prints, the registrar's condition line, the unclaimed-burden determination, and five board lines, two of which restate positions settled in Ch 28–36 — ten records. Logged as a deliberate overrun in `continuity.md`; each is load-bearing for a later beat, including Councillor Sore's speech, so none was cut. Volume 02 must not exceed one notice in an ordinary chapter.**) There is a fifty-eight in the crossing log for the night of the surge with the word *both* under it.

**19:10, a car park in the Old Ward. Oren Pell's record is not a minute** — a minute is a list, a record describes a state of a node. He reads the condition line into the record: **IF THE FLAP IS NOT SHUT, THE BANK IS NOT A WALL.** For the file, and not for Jonas, he enters that it was available on 1 April and was not. **The cost of reading it in is that a civic work with no holder and no issuer is an unclaimed burden, and it attaches to the nearest responsible maintenance route, and the department had already given Jonas one, and so it attaches to the interim lowland post instead: HOLDER, ROYDEN ACHEBE.** Achebe accepts at about 19:06 from a yard in Gantry Ward in nine words, and says he would rather have had it before he had the post. **Tomas Renn's submission is in the public papers at 09:14 that morning with a minority report, and a CivicCore officer telephones the shop about a joint working paper, and Tomas says no in a sentence and tells nobody.**

**Saturday 19 April, 07:30, the towpath.** **Councillor Iven Sore comes up because Jonas sent her the tide sheet, and Aurrie Nkemdirim decides she comes up.** She gets eleven minutes. She tells them she wanted the contract passed in June, and that the water came, and that she was at home, and that eleven people were in a Methodist hall at six in the morning with no heating. She is handed the contractor's retrospective participation offer and **Yasmin Rohan says no in the same sentence she said it on 31 March**, and eleven people in that kitchen who had not heard the first no found it strange. Then Sore asks the question — *who is the other end of it* — and the answer given back to her is that on the other end of it there is nobody, not because people will not answer, but because a department cannot give a record of a place with no address, and if it could the record would be a form with a number in it, and the number would be the wrong one. **Sore does not look at the ring of pencil on the wall. Jonas understands that not looking was the most important thing that happened in that kitchen and that he was the only person who could tell it had happened.** She says she is not going to turn round in a kitchen and be a different person in a chamber, and that she does not think the contract should be passed in June any more, and will not explain why in there.

### Chapter 0047 — The Map Redraws Itself

**Friday 18 April, 11 p.m., the Old Ward basement.** The four-sheet service diagram is printed off the Lattice's own drawing layer. It shows **nine ordinary service districts** with names, filled holder squares, expiries and node numbers — a borough, a trust, a company, a hospital, a school, a transit operator, a housing association, a market, a utilities district. A service district is a shape drawn out of the terms in force inside it and is not an administrative ward; the nine are not the city's eight districts and never were. **And in the middle of the middle there is nothing: a blank square the size of a thumbnail with no name, no number, no node and no line in the key.** The basement printer says it is a root field, it has no holder, and root fields are not drawn.

**Inside the blank square there is a faint closed outline, an empty holder square, a dot, and one line in the smallest type: DRAWN FROM A TERM IN FORCE 16:40, 17 APRIL TO 06:00, 18 APRIL. NO HOLDER FIELD. NO NAME. NO CLASSIFICATION.** That term was issued in a net-loft kitchen in four minutes for a door, a stove, a floor and a woman of seventy-seven with a chest. **The term lasted thirteen hours and twenty minutes and Lowglass is legally unlisted.** The print was pulled at about 23:00, **seventeen hours after the term had ended**, so the shape was not a stale copy of an earlier state: **the drawing layer has no delete operation, and it drew a shape out of a term that had already lapsed and annotated its own error underneath. The outline was on the layer for at least seventeen hours after the term ended and is still on it.** The one notice, true and being broken by the same machine: **THE MAP IS DRAWN FROM THE TERMS IN FORCE. A DISTRICT WITH NO TERM IS NOT DRAWN.** Nobody printed a second copy and nobody thought there was anything to print.

**Saturday 19 April, 10:55, committee room two, four days' notice, gallery of forty-one.** The determination is **suspended pending determination** — not a dissolution, and CivicCore keeps the physical network. The unclaimed burden is noted and the committee **cannot act**. **Councillor Sore moves the adjournment herself, says it is not in the interest of her own party, and the committee adjourns to 19 May because it cannot report on a contract that turns on *holder* until it has determined what a holder is.** The district shown unnamed inside the blank centre is recorded as appearing, **no holder field is created, no name is entered**, a claim may be stated in a box on the board for ninety days and a claim so stated may not be used as evidence of occupancy, population, households or persons.

**And then the register of attachment**, moved by Nina Calder as records, drafted twice. **MERCER, J. — LAST WORKING SYSTEM ADMINISTRATOR, CITY OF NACRE. Civic standing, not departmental. Not subject to review, grade or probation. Bears unclaimed burdens and claims attached in his own name, findable by the city, released by no person.** Oren Pell states the reason and has it minuted: the register gives him no root access, no precedence, no office, no staff, no vote, no key and no right to refuse an attachment, and it puts his name where the city can find it so that claims land in the open instead of in a file, and it makes the next person who wants to attach a burden look at a register first and see he is already the one. ***It is a bucket under a tap, and the tap is going to run for a long time.*** Marsh refuses to minute it until the basis is stated. **Jonas is in the gallery and was not asked, and asks one question — can it be taken off — and the answer is no.** Pell starts to describe the three residents the burden would otherwise have gone to; **Councillor Sore stops him in front of a full gallery, he complies, and the minute is corrected** to *the nearest person who can be reached other than a resident of an unnamed district.* In the corridor, **Alden Rook** says the clause comes back on 19 May, that the first person who can define *holder* controls every node in the city, that in about two years somebody will use this register to say the city has an administrator and he will have been right in a corridor at half past two with nobody on his side, and then: **do not lose the map.**

### Chapter 0048 — Permission: Find the Blank Bearer

**Saturday 19 April, 06:10, the Weir stand.** The term expired at six and the plate went quiet, and there is no sentence in any manual in the city for ten past six on a Saturday. **Bo Trimble reads the five lines aloud to Margo Doré and reads the claim out loud first, and says it is not true** — the claim is that the holder keeps the point true until the silt trap is clear, and the trap is not clear. Margo Doré says a plate does not care whether a claim is true and a person does, and that the difference is the entire argument of this street. **She takes it for one window: 06:00 Saturday to 06:00 Sunday.** The rota for the twenty-four hours is one name and no names under it, every draw is entered with a time and not with whose, and **nobody on that street will be able to prove in June that they had water**, which is the price of not having a list, and she says so before anybody asks. The bank line has a ring round a blank space and nothing in it.

**The shop, Saturday.** The tool roll is too heavy for one person. **Dev Singh takes the far end, carries it eleven feet, says the west lane is clear and the two handovers are in the book, and goes out to his car.** Bea Osunde reads the register of attachment twice, puts it face down, says she is not going to say *never mind* or *I am sorry* or *we will manage*, says that the line *released by no person* is either a wall or a floor and she is not going to work it out for him, and tells him to go and do his round.

**The round, Sunday on foot**: the market hatch opening and staying open and Fola Adeyemi not turning round; Kofi Sarr reporting the third asking and the dinner he has refused; Ibrahim Sesay's one-line procedure in a gate book and the line that is not there for him; Emeka Rusk on a gantry above a wheel on a Sunday, saying *let the box stay blank*. At Saint Orra west the Sunday corridor is empty with the plate saying four true words, and at 22:00 Ezra reads the five lines to Ruth and **Mina Okafor reads them back a second time, because it is her job, and says so twice to two different people.** The plate over the card table carries **ISSUER: MERCER, J.**, which is the only thing on it with his name on, and **HOLDER: THE HOLDER ON DUTY**, which has no name in the field and four names on a board two feet away. The public box now reads nineteen minutes, forty minutes, and a live term. **The retainer line is struck through in Selah Mora's own hand, in front of a clerk, at 15:20 on 18 April, nine days after Mina Okafor stopped — and Selah says she is not going to ask her to come back, because a witness who comes back out of politeness is a witness you can lose twice.** The ramp handrail is sound and he does not write it up. Three people are waiting at the gate at six and two of the three answers are *I do not know*.

**Sunday evening, the bench.** The map has been in the rain on the crate and **there is a name written across the middle of it in biro, pressing hard, over the blank square, in a hand that is not his.** He reads it twice and does not know it. He folds the sheets with the margin inward and does not ask anybody, because asking a street whose only power is that there is nothing on any list which name is on his map is how a name becomes a record. He carries the tool roll to the Old Ward porch in two trips because there is nobody in the shop to take the other end, and sweeps the water out of the porch, and the board is lit and carries **CENTRAL ROOT — HOLDER: —** and the new diagram and an empty claims box that a man of sixty used and put back in his coat. And on the enamel strip at the frame, unsigned, since a quarter past seven:

**Permission: find the blank bearer.**

## Volume 02 — The Price of Access

### Chapter 0049 — Not a Service

**Monday 21 April, morning into afternoon.
