It is Prue Calloway's finding of 23 April, and he is not going to say what he thinks it means, and he says that he has wanted to since August and that **there is a fourth page of a regulation from 2008 that three people in this borough have read and told nobody, that he is not one of the three and is not going to say who the three are, and that he is not going to say what is on it in a room with nine people in it, and he wants that minuted as a decision and not as a virtue.** The minute has no box for the name of a person and records that nothing in the item requires any person to do anything, and it went out on the Friday and contains no names at all. **The term in force was read at ten o'clock on Saturday 24 January and expires at six o'clock on Sunday 1 February, and there is no date written down anywhere for that Saturday night, and a term is six days and it is read on the Saturday night before the Sunday it starts on, so one Saturday night is a week after the Saturday night before it, and there has been one of those every week since the first week of April — 31 January, 7 February, 14 February, 21 February, 28 February — and the seventh of them falls on Saturday 7 March 2015, and there is no date on any piece of paper in this city for the seventh of them, or the first, or any of the five in between, because a person is not required to be told, and there is nobody in this borough who is required to write it.**

## Volume 03, Batch 0003 — Chapters 121–130

**Chapter 0121 — The Same Eleven Seconds. Monday 2 February 2015, the vestibule, ground floor, the Old Ward. Jonas Mercer asks no question; the only question in the room is a man of fifty-eight's, and it is three words: `Is it drinkable.` ABU BAKAYOKO, 58, comes back seven days after the twenty-sixth of January because the trough by the lock-ups came back on the Friday night, is not frozen, and has about two inches of something green in it that is not water, and he has to decide by lunchtime whether to fill from it. She says the same eleven seconds are not available: what she can be held to is a state on a date, and a date is a thing about a time, and a trough is a place and a place does not hold still. She gives him a second date on a second slip and says out loud that it is the first time she has written the same date twice and that in about four years there will be a shelf, and a shelf of them is a list of states, and a list of states is a schedule, and a schedule is the thing this borough has been refusing since the first of March. She keeps a copy of each in a drawer under the counter; there is no key and no list, **and she gives out loud the reason that drawer is not a shelf, which is that a piece of paper only gets written when a person stands in front of her and asks what was true on a day, and it is remarkable, and it is a little frightening, how few of them do.** JONAS'S FINDING, in the corridor, in about eleven minutes, and it is the engine of the batch: A STATE GIVEN ON A TUESDAY CAN BE WRONG ON THE WEDNESDAY, and a counter that is required to answer and is not required to make one will go on answering the same state for as long as nobody comes back to argue it; there is no obligation in this city to come back; a person turning up is not an instrument, it is a coincidence, and a coincidence every week is a service. And the third thing: a shelf grows the way the register grew, out of correct things done by careful people, nobody is hurt by it, and in about four years somebody will hold one over somebody, and it will not be the bulb. He did not ask how many there are, and she is not going to tell, and the back of her hand is not a book.**

**Chapter 0122 — The Man Who Is Safer Not Knowing. Wednesday 4 February into Friday 6 February 2015, the records office second floor and a ground-floor corridor at Saint Orra west. Jonas asks NO QUESTION IN EITHER ROOM, and the chapter says so as its finding. The autumn compliance report is public and carries PARAGRAPH 4.11 — decontamination, west site, level zero; the historical half of the weekly verification record cannot be reconstructed and the non-conformance is carried forward. There is no name in it because there has never been a name field on the sheet the paragraphs are counted from, and the log is a supplier's grid of fifty-two Sundays with a tick in a temperature box for four books and a signature box empty on every line. Four hundred and twenty women have instruments out of that room every weekday and every one of them is on the far side of a paragraph number. Dr Sanaa Iqbal refused twenty minutes a week in her own words in May and has not been asked since. THE BATCH'S MOST EXACT REFUSAL, said out loud once in a corridor to BRAM OSEI, a porter: `BECAUSE IF I TELL HIM, THEN HE IS THE PERSON WHO KNOWS, AND A PERSON WHO KNOWS IS A PERSON SOMEBODY CAN ASK, AND THE ONLY INSTRUMENT IN THIS BOROUGH THAT KEEPS A MAN FROM BEING ASKABLE IS A MAN WHO DOES NOT KNOW. HE IS SAFER NOT KNOWING.` Bram Osei agrees, says it is also true about him, and it is the first time in this account that the correct action has been to leave a person in ignorance on purpose. NINETY SECONDS: the report stands, the paragraph stands, four hundred and twenty women stand behind a paragraph number, and nobody has been harmed and nobody has been helped, and the chapter says that out loud and does not apologise for it. ONE LATTICE NOTICE IN THE BATCH, on the wall by the lift: the machine keeps its own cycle record on a strip, the strip goes in the drawer in the room, the quarterly return is made by the supplier, no person signs this sheet. Nothing defines *holder*. HOOK: a strip is a piece of paper, a drawer is a place, and a piece of paper does not put itself in a drawer — so somebody has been putting strips in that drawer about forty times a day and that person has not been found either.**

**Chapter 0123 — What Comes Back To A Counter. Friday 6 February into Monday 9 February 2015, the vestibule. Jonas asks no question. THE CLOSE POV IS PERMITTED HERE AND IT IS ON A MAN WHO IS NOT ON ANY BOARD AND IS NOT PAID: EMMANUEL OTIENO, 38, agency ten years, four agencies in nine years, nights at a depot four to ten, rents a top floor, a daughter of nine who walks to a bus stop at the end of his road, not on any list, nothing in any file in this city. He has been to a counter five times in his life. He is given a state and a date about the tap on the wall of the yard where he parks the van, and refused in about eleven seconds on the bus stop, and he is not angry, and a counter is required to answer and is not required to go and look, and those are two different jobs and this borough has money for neither of them and a person for one of them. HIS ELEVEN WORDS ON THE WAY OUT THE DOOR: `A person can ask a question here and get a date.` CLOSE POV, in a kitchen, alone, on a Sunday: the difference is not the answer — a date is a thing about a piece of paper, and a date can be wrong, and if it is wrong you can come back — and then the danger: HE CAN NOW DO THIS TO ANYTHING. He has a plastic wallet in the glovebox of a van he does not own with more than one piece of paper in it, and nobody in this city can say how many, and he has shown it to nobody, and he felt something at a sink that he did not have a word for and the word he did not have a word for was powerful. JONAS'S FINDING: a number of nine in a record is a capacity, a capacity is a promise, a level of service is a thing a person may also be given LESS of, and the day there is a number on that counter there is a morning somebody can say *we can only do eight today* and be entirely correct and not be lying. HOOK: a letter asks whether the counter can be put on the borough's website, and there is no website, and the same letter says `I am not asking for a list of people who use it and I would like it recorded that I have not asked for that`, and Calder says to a tray: in four years somebody will ask for the second one, and it will not be a person who is ashamed of asking.**

**Chapter 0124 — The Second Asker. Wednesday 11 February into Friday 13 February 2015, a corridor on the second floor and the vestibule. Jonas asks no question. ABU BAKAYOKO comes back with the copy of the minute of 15 January in a plastic wallet and reads `as a statement and not as a return` out loud twice. THE MINUTE DOES NOT BIND ANYBODY, DOES NOT REQUIRE ANYTHING, AND CANNOT BE PRODUCED AT A WATER POINT, and a minute that says a person asked and names nobody cannot be put beside a bowser in February. THE FINDING ABOUT WHY IT IS THE SECOND ASKER: a man who asked once could still believe the record was an accident, and four weeks is long enough to stop believing that; a second asker is a man who has run out of accident, and he is right to, and there is no arrangement in this borough that can make him not right to. HER NINE SECONDS: it is a statement, and it stands, and nobody may take it off; it is not a return; a return goes to somebody who has to do something about it, and there is no somebody. AND THEN SHE OFFERS SOMETHING — the first thing anybody in this borough has offered anybody in about four months, worth nothing, and it works, and it is not a service: A SECOND DATE, ON A SECOND SLIP, SAYING WHAT WAS TRUE ON THAT DATE, SIGNED BY NOBODY, KEPT BY A PERSON IN A VESTIBULE ELEVEN FEET BY NINE. HE WOULD HAVE IT BACK ON ANY MORNING. A MAN WHO CAN BE WRONG IN PUBLIC IS NOT A MAN WHO HAS TO BE RIGHT IN A VAN. He is not going to get his Tuesday back; nobody is; what he has got is the ability to be wrong in public on a date. The interval: three slips mention that trough and the gap that matters is 2 February to 13 February, ELEVEN DAYS, and eleven days has no word, and a series has a word for the gap, and there is no form in this borough on which anybody could write that these are not a series.**

**Chapter 0125 — A Reason Column, Again, And Why There Is No Eighth. Monday 16 February into Wednesday 18 February 2015, the vestibule. It is NOT M. FURNEAUX, who wrote once on 2 January, was refused, and is not going to write again before about 2018. It is HOWARD BRAMBLE, 61, fourth floor of a block of forty flats in Tideglass, nineteen months, a wife who cannot carry anything heavier than a kettle, not on any list, who went and asked for the unsigned extract himself in January. He got a slip about a standpipe on 5 February; it ran on the Thursday and did not run on the Saturday, and being wrong cost him nothing, and there was no reason in the world for him to think of the counter on the Friday. He asks for a REASON — one line, free text, against each of the nineteen entries, not a drop-down, not a category, in the hand of the person who decided it, dated — and says first that he is not asking for a name, and that the bulb is out and he can see that from his car park, so that one is honest. HIS ARGUMENT IS THE BEST MADE IN THIS BOROUGH SINCE MARCH: you will give me a date and you will not give me a reason, and both are the same shape — a line of free text, in a hand, on a date — and the only reason the counter can do one and not the other is that the first is about a thing and the second is about a person. Jonas asks his one question in the room, nine words: `Is that the best argument anybody has made here.` She answers: it is, and it is a measurement and not a compliment, and he has written it down twice. SHE REFUSES IN THE RIGHT ORDER IN ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS AND IT STANDS AND IT IS NOT THE EIGHTH, BECAUSE A LIST IS NINETEEN ENTRIES WITH SOMETHING AGAINST EACH OF THEM AND YOU CAN COUNT IT, and this is a thing asked for twice and told no twice, and if she writes a number against it she has made a list of people who think the board is dangerous, and in about four years that list is a drawer. SHE SAYS OUT LOUD, IN A ROOM, IN DAYLIGHT: **IT IS NOT A LIST.** Five words in biro on the back of the left hand of the man of about fifty-six, who is not a member and is not on any list, in about four seconds, at the wall; nobody saw him and nobody has asked him what is on his hand. HER ANSWER, and it is the price: if the reason is going to exist anywhere it is going to exist on a slip from a counter with a date on it and nobody's name on it, about a thing and not about a decision, and that is a very poor document and it will settle nothing, and she would rather it did not exist at all. HIS ANSWER BACK: then in about four years there is going to be nothing at all to put on the table for a frightened person in a kitchen, and he is sixty-one. FINDING: A REQUEST CAN BE REFUSED TWICE AND THE SECOND REFUSAL IS CHEAPER — by the second time both of them know what it is for — and it cost her about nine minutes instead of eleven seconds. HOOK: a letter into the records office on Wednesday 18 February at about half past nine, not on borough stock, a real departmental reference, about four lines, and it is not a reason column and it is the only thing anybody in this borough has wanted since the first of March that is smaller than a list.**

**Chapter 0126 — The Two Conditions, Sixteen Inches Apart. Friday 20 February into Monday 23 February 2015, the ground-floor corridor. Jonas asks ONE question in the vestibule on the Friday, eight words — `Do either of those apply to this counter` — and one on the Monday, and he volunteers nothing further and gets the second halves of both answers unasked. Eight people cross the sixteen inches in two days and not one of them stops, and one of them puts a hand up in front of her face. THE FINDING IS PHYSICAL AND NOT MORAL: a person walking under two conditions that do not agree goes FASTER, not slower. If there were one condition a person would obey it, refuse it or stop, and all three are slow and all three are visible to the person behind them, and a second condition that disagrees takes all three off the table. What is left is walking. A CONTRADICTION IS NOT A PAUSE. A CONTRADICTION IS A FLOOR THAT IS NOT LEVEL, AND EVERYBODY CROSSES A FLOOR THAT IS NOT LEVEL FASTER THAN A FLOOR THAT IS. Timed at about three miles an hour and about four, a difference of about a second and a half, about four times a day on the two days he watched, which is not a rate. THE REGISTRAR'S SENTENCE OF 2 JUNE: a clause that cannot be challenged by a person cannot require them to. And the corollary: A PERSON WHO GOES FASTER UNDER A WALL OF TWO CONTRADICTORY CONDITIONS HAS BEEN REQUIRED TO DO SOMETHING BY NEITHER OF THEM. Her answer, both halves, volunteered: nothing on that wall governs this counter, and if it did this counter would be a service; and somebody is going to walk in and say *that is the rules*, and they will not be lying, and one of the two will be right, and the other one will still be on the wall — and she refused, unasked, to say which one, and gave the reason, and the reason is the same reason he gave in a room on 28 January about a page of a regulation from 2008, and if he asks on the second of June she will tell him the same thing in the same words. She would rather stand under a contradiction for another four years than be the woman who settled it. JONAS'S FINDING: THE FLOOR OF A SERVICE IS A PLACE WHERE A PERSON CAN STAND UNDER A CONTRADICTION AND BE ANSWERED, and he is not going to write it on a wall. HOOK: there is a card in the slot for post, four inches by two, folded once, not a form, no crest, no reference, no box, no envelope, put in on the Friday afternoon or the Saturday; nobody has taken it out and nobody has put it up; she has not read it and is not going to, and a slot that gets emptied is a rule, and a rule is a service, and hers is the name it would have. `I am not leaving it in it. I am not taking it out of it. Those are different, and the difference is the whole of what this room is.`**

**Chapter 0127 — The Man Who Could Be Asked, Still. Wednesday 25 February into Friday 27 February 2015, the counter on the second floor and the shop. Jonas asks ONE question at the counter, five words — `Does a correction go anywhere` — to the woman who has been at that counter for eleven years and is not Nina Calder. The five routes to Royden Achebe have not changed since 22 December and every one of them is a document and a person who will remember it. WHAT CHANGED IN FEBRUARY IS NOT ACHEBE. IT IS THE PRICE: a counter in this borough now mints findability, and findability used to cost a day and a raincoat and a decision, and a date is the cheapest handle in this city. HIS ARITHMETIC, four days to write down: a date is a state with a day on it; a date about a trough costs eleven seconds and a slip and is worth exactly that; a date about him saying a man's name in a room on 21 November would cost him about the same — and that is the problem. The woman's answer, both halves: a correction is not a document, not a return, not a minute, not a file, no box would take it, in the post it becomes a letter, and a letter is a thing that exists in a building that keeps post, and in about four years somebody can say that a man with civic standing and no grade wrote to a manager on a date and cannot say what was in it, and that is exactly why they will say it. `If I could make it go nowhere, it would be worthless. If I could make it go somewhere, it would be a record. Those are the same door and it only opens one way, and the way it opens is into a file.` He did not go to Gantry Ward and did not telephone and did not use the switchboard and did not stand on a landing. BEATRICE OSUNDE, in the shop, does not argue and does not improve it and does not tell him he is brave: a man who can be asked one question about anything, for ever, by anybody, is not findable, he is available, and a handle is cheap; A RECEIPT IS NOT AN APOLOGY, AND THE WHOLE OF WHAT HE HAS BEEN PROTECTING THAT MAN FROM FOR EIGHT MONTHS IS A BILL. And the sentence the batch ends on, said once, to the bench oiling: **YOU HAVE BEEN FINDING PEOPLE FOR EIGHT MONTHS AND YOU HAVE NOT YET FOUND OUT THAT THE EXPENSIVE HALF IS NOT BEING FOUND. IT IS BEING ASKED.** FINDING: FINDABILITY AND ASKABILITY ARE TWO DIFFERENT CURRENCIES AND THIS BOROUGH HAS BEEN MINTING THE FIRST ONE VERY SUCCESSFULLY SINCE THE THIRD OF APRIL AND HAS NOT SPENT ONE UNIT OF THE SECOND ONE AT ALL. HOOK: PETER NAYLOR, 44, has a fortnight somewhere in this city with a bucket under a tap and it came round at the end of February, it has been four degrees all week, there is a frost warning out for the twenty-eighth, and there is nobody in this borough who is able to ask him whether he has got a coat.**

**Chapter 0128 — A Saturday Night Nobody Wrote Down. Monday 2 March into Wednesday 4 March 2015, a ground-floor corridor at Saint Orra west and the records office. Jonas asks one question at the counter on the Wednesday, ten words — `Does anything the records office holds bear on that gate` — and no question at all in the corridor at the hospital, having told the porter in advance he would not ask him one. HE HAD BEEN IN THAT CORRIDOR ONCE, at half past four in the morning on Sunday 4 January 2015, for the last hour and a half of a term. He did not go on the Saturday night before it and stood four hundred yards away in the rain and did not telephone, because ringing is the same as going. And on 27 December, in a different building and for an entirely different reason, he stood on a first landing outside a fire door for about ten minutes and did not use the keypad. Three decisions, all different. AND THEN THE FIRST OF FEBRUARY CAME AND WENT AND HE DID NOT GO AND HE DID NOT DECIDE NOT TO GO, AND THERE WAS NOTHING ON ANY DAY OF THAT WEEK THAT SAID A MAN HAD CHOSEN ANYTHING. That was the fourth one. That is what a fourth one is. A fourth is a habit and a habit is a rota with one name in it, and in about four years that is not a memory, that is a duty. On Monday 2 March at about eleven in the morning, in daylight, on foot, off the street, the way anybody walks into a hospital, he told BRAM OSEI out loud, on purpose, that he was not going to be in that room on Saturday 7 March. Bram Osei's answer: `Then I shall not say that you were there, and I shall not say that you were not there, and I have not said it to anybody, and a thing said by two people in a corridor is a thing that two people know, and I do not want to be two.` He has told nobody. The cost of it: he made a porter a person who knows something, on purpose, having told that porter twenty-four days earlier, on the sixth of February, that the only instrument that keeps a man from being askable is a man who does not know; he said so and did not apologise, and Bram Osei agreed it was worse, and it is the only thing he has said in this account that is actually about himself. WHO READ THEM ALOUD, on every handover since 3 January: EZRA MBEKI, in and out of that building since 1992, and TREVOR NUNN, 58, a night porter, nineteen years, not on a roster because the roster does not exist. The term in force was read at 22:00 on Saturday 28 February, began 07:00 Sunday 1 March and expires 06:00 Sunday 8 March, and the handover in front of that one is at ten o'clock on Saturday 7 March, in five days. NOTHING WAS WRITTEN DOWN. The eleven-row clipboard is on the fourth chair and he has not touched it. A counter in this borough was asked nothing about the gate and looked for nothing, and the reason given out loud is that a counter with an opinion about a gate is a service. THE FINDING: THE MAN WAS NEVER IN IT. THE GATE HAS NEVER ONCE BEEN OPENED BY ANYTHING HE DID IN A CORRIDOR AT HALF PAST FOUR IN THE MORNING, AND THE ONLY REASON HE WAS EVER THERE IS THAT HE WAS NOT REQUIRED TO BE ANYWHERE. That decision cost nothing and it is the only thing in nine months he has got right by not doing it. HOOK: there is a Saturday night coming on 7 March 2015, the seventh of them in six weeks, and there is no date on it, and no form in this borough on which anybody could fill in to find out whether it happened.**

**Chapter 0129 — A Service Floor, In February. Friday 6 March into Monday 9 March 2015, the vestibule. Jonas asks ONE question, five words — `What is this counter for` — and she refuses it in three parts, in the right order, and the third part is the whole of it: A COUNTER THAT CAN BE ASKED WHAT IT IS FOR IS A COUNTER THAT HAS BEEN ASKED TO BE SOMETHING, AND THE DAY IT HAS BEEN ASKED TO BE SOMETHING IT IS A SERVICE. The inventory: the register at nineteen, the blank line blank and a hundred days old, the bulb still out, the two conditions sixteen inches apart, the card in the slot. THE DRAWER UNDER THE COUNTER IS NOT LOCKED AND THE EDGES OF A STACK ARE VISIBLE IN IT AND HE HAS NOT COUNTED THEM AND HAS NOT LOOKED AND HAS NOT ASKED. There are nine. One hand, a date on each, no reference, no number, no crest, no form, no box, and no name on any of them including hers. **AND NINE SLIPS AND ELEVEN PEOPLE A DAY ARE TWO DIFFERENT NUMBERS AND ARE NOT A CONTRADICTION: a slip is only written when somebody asks what was true on a day, and out of nine people a day, and then eleven, the ones who ask that are three men who have all come more than once and one other person about whom nobody has said a word, and that is the whole of them, and the two must never be added together, and a form that added them would say *this counter sees eleven people and produces one document*, which is a level of service. Eight of the nine went into three inside pockets; the ninth went out on a Friday afternoon in the last week of February to somebody who was not in a room with anybody.** THE THROUGHPUT IS ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE A DAY WHERE IT WAS ABOUT NINE, on the back of a hand, and a number a person can be held to is a level of service and the day there is a level of service somebody can cut it. **She gives neither number to the man in the rain when he asks her why she does this at all: she describes the drawer and says nothing about how many are in it.** At about ten to eleven a man of fifty comes in out of the rain and asks whether that is where you go if you want to know about a person, and asks for a state about his sister, and is told no: there is nothing in this borough that can tell him where she is, there is no form on any counter that would take his question, and if there were one then in about four years there would be a list of sisters. HE IS NOT ANGRY. He asks why she does this at all, and she answers in about eleven seconds, and nobody asked her to, and it is the only thing anybody in this borough has ever said that was both free and a use: BECAUSE THERE ARE NINE PIECES OF PAPER IN THE DRAWER UNDER THIS COUNTER AND EVERY ONE OF THEM HAS A DATE ON IT AND A HAND ON IT AND NO NAME ON IT. And: THE COUNTER WORKS BECAUSE A PERSON IS ON IT, AND SHE IS NOT FINDABLE, AND SHE IS NOT ON ANYTHING, AND SHE COMES DOWN TWICE A DAY BECAUSE NOBODY HAS EVER TOLD HER TO AND NOBODY COULD, AND IF SHE STOPPED COMING DOWN THERE WOULD BE A COUNTER AND THERE WOULD NOT BE A COUNTER. He said: that is the answer to my question and I did not ask it. She said: no, you asked me why I do this and I have told you about a drawer. THE ASKING WAS RECORDED BY NOBODY. THE NOT-ANSWERING WAS CORRECT. He went out into the rain with nothing at all. The eleventh thing, the first anybody has added, and it is not an item and it is in no minute and it is not going on a wall: **THE FLOOR CAN BE ASKED ABOUT ITSELF, AND THE ONLY REASON IT CAN BE ASKED ABOUT ITSELF IS THAT THERE IS A PERSON ON IT AND THE PERSON IS NOT FINDABLE.** On the Monday Jonas does not ask her anything, and there is a letter sitting in a tray on the second floor that has been sitting there since 18 February that he has not been told about and she has not told him about and that is not going to come up in this room.**

**Chapter 0130 — What Is On The Floor, In February. Wednesday 11 March into Friday 13 March 2015, the vestibule, in daylight, about eleven people standing sideways, and there is no chair and there is no chair of that body and there never was. Jonas asks NO QUESTION on either day. The room does what it does and says what is true in about nine minutes: the lowland cannot be seated and the reason was not fixed; the board stands at seven of nine with two chairs; four of the nine have said they will stop if it turns into a job and one has given a reason and asked for it not to be written down and it is not in the minute; a man of forty-four cannot be asked anything, ever; a man of forty-nine can be asked one question tomorrow morning and has not been; a woman of thirty-four is in about eleven people and in no document and nobody has been to the bottom of that street since 19 January; a woman of seventy-two is in room 14 and is a great deal more frightened and no more findable; a woman of sixty-one is on no board with nine hundred and eighty-six Wednesdays on a card under a rubber band, and it is a number of Wednesdays and not a number of people; and the ninth seat is fifty-four and unpaid and findable at a shopfront and nobody in that room has ever told her and nobody is going to. At about twenty past eleven on the Friday the man of about fifty-six asked the second question — the one that is not what it is made of. **What it costs.** And Jonas answers, and the answer is a sentence and not a scheme and it is not the sentence anybody expected: **A SERVICE FLOOR COSTS THE PEOPLE WHO ARE ON IT AND NOT THE PEOPLE WHO USE IT. THE PRICE IS FINDABILITY. EVERY PERSON IN THIS BOROUGH WHO HAS EVER STOOD AT THAT COUNTER IS NOW SLIGHTLY EASIER TO FIND THAN THEY WERE, AND NONE OF THEM WAS ASKED, AND EVERY SINGLE ONE OF THE REASONS IT IS BETTER IS A REAL REASON AND NONE OF THE REASONS IT IS WORSE IS A COMPLAINT.** It is better because a person can be wrong in public on a date, and being wrong in public on a date is worth more than being right in a van; and it is worse because a person who is findable is a person who can be asked. Then the specific people and each one's price, and the closing sentence: NOBODY HAS BEEN ASKED WHETHER THEY CAN AFFORD THAT, AND THIS BOROUGH HAS NO INSTRUMENT FOR ASKING, AND THE ONLY INSTRUMENT IT HAS EVER HAD IS A FORM, AND A FORM HAS A BOX ON IT, AND THE BOX IS WHERE THE ANSWER WOULD GO, AND A PERSON'S ANSWER TO *CAN YOU AFFORD TO BE FOUND* DOES NOT FIT IN A BOX AND IF IT DID IT WOULD NOT BE TRUE. The minute went out on the Friday, is public, and contains no names, and nothing in it requires any person to do anything. THE ROLL-CALL: the register at nineteen and the blank line blank for a hundred and seven days; the September record public, the determination public, the quarterly public with its number of times and its source in the same paragraph; the second record out, 51 nodes, 38 parties, 13 shapes, unsigned, no name box, about four hundred names, eleven extracts, two wrong write-backs and *enclosure received, no comment* in the file; the reading rule in one woman's inside pocket and used once on a man of forty-four who does not know; seven of nine; the letter worked and its price a sheet of A4 on a door and a woman more frightened; six words his and two men findable because he said things in rooms; a woman's name in eleven people and in no document; a letter in a tray nobody has opened; and the winter's one Sunday in every week, with no date written down for any of them. AND THE TWO THINGS LEFT OPEN ON PURPOSE: Prue Calloway's finding of 23 April 2014, stated once in this room six weeks ago by a man with no standing to state it, in a minute, unresolved, and it may not be opened again — WHAT IS LEFT OF IT IS ITS CONSEQUENCE, AND NOBODY IN THIS BOROUGH HAS BEEN MADE TO LIVE UNDER THE CONSEQUENCE YET AND NOBODY IS GOING TO BE — and a Saturday night at ten o'clock, the seventh of them in six weeks having fallen on 7 March, with the gate up at seven on Sunday 8 March because two people who are not on anything were in a room with four chairs the night before, and another one coming on Saturday 14 March which will be the eighth in seven weeks, and there is no date on it and no form on which anybody could record that it happened, AND NOBODY HAS WRITTEN IT DOWN. Volume 03 is not closed by this chapter.**

## Volume 03, Batch 0004 — Chapters 131–140

**Chapter 0131 — A Thing That Is True Twice. Saturday 14 March 2015 at ten o'clock at night, a waiting area off a corridor on level two, Saint Orra west; and Monday 16 March 2015, the vestibule, ground floor, the Old Ward. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION in either room and there was one question of somebody else's, in a public room, at about half past ten. THE ONLY HANDOVER STAGED IN THE WHOLE BATCH: a term read at 22:00 on Saturday the fourteenth of March, the eighth of them in seven weeks, in a room with four chairs and a machine for hot water that has not worked since about August, by EZRA MBEKI and TREVOR NUNN, 58, a night porter, nineteen years, who is not on a roster because the roster does not exist. NOTHING WAS WRITTEN DOWN. The term runs six days, began at seven o'clock on Sunday the fifteenth and expired at six o'clock on Sunday the twenty-second. JONAS MERCER WAS FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY, ON PURPOSE, DECIDING IT ON THE FRIDAY, AND NEITHER MBEKI NOR NUNN KNOWS THAT AND NEITHER IS GOING TO BE TOLD. On the Monday morning a man of about sixty with a galvanised can of about ten litres, who has never been in that vestibule in his life, asked WHAT WAS IN THE TROUGH BY THE LOCK-UPS ON THE SECOND OF FEBRUARY, and she answered in about eleven seconds in three halves, and the state was right about the second of February and WRONG ABOUT THE TROUGH, and the wrongness is not a fault of the counter — it is a fact about a Tuesday. She gave a state about a man standing in front of her with a bowser to fill and a lunchtime to decide by; the green in that trough was forty winters of market waste settling out of roof water; what he put in himself was not what made it green, and she would not say that to him as a fact because a records counter with an opinion about a trough is a service. He said `Then I will not put the drum in` and went out with the can and the answer and nobody was hurt and nobody was helped. THE FOUR LINES IN THE PAD: a state that has gone stale belongs to a THING and a thing can be looked at; a state that has been contradicted belongs to a PERSON and cannot be looked at, because only the man who said it can contradict it, and he has not come back and nobody can ask him to, and a person may not be required to answer anything including a correction. ONE HAS AN OWNER AND THE OTHER HAS A PERSON, AND THIS BOROUGH HAS NO INSTRUMENT FOR EITHER. A slip is a record of a person's account of a thing, not a record of the thing. BEA OSUNDE, in the shop, did not improve it: he cannot go and find the people who were there on a day and ask them whether they are sure, because a person standing in a corridor asking another person about a day becomes, in about four years, a list of the days people were sure about, with his name on the top of it as the man who asked. THE HOOK, THE BATCH'S LIVE FURNITURE: a thing under the post in a tray on the second floor, in there since Wednesday the eighteenth of February, twenty-six days, and somebody has started to leave post on top of it, which is the oldest way in the world of saying a thing has not been dealt with. The register at nineteen and the blank line blank for a hundred and ten days. The card in the slot since 20 February. Nine slips in a drawer and about eleven people a day, and the two were never added.**

**Chapter 0132 — The Man Who Turned The Bucket. Wednesday 18 March into Friday 20 March 2015, a corridor, ground floor, the Old Ward, and a shop at the end of a street. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION in either place. The whole of what anybody knows about the man who turns the bucket on the board of forty slots at a bus turning head is one sentence said out loud in a room in daylight on 13 January by a man of fifty-eight, and the sentence ends: `and I could not tell you his name either.` He is not findable from any record in this city and has never been, and the reason there is no record of him is not that he was missed. It is that nothing in this borough has ever had to write down a man who turns a bucket. THE STAND IS IN PARAGRAPH 6.1 OF THE MINUTE OF 19 MAY with a number in it, and a man in a haversack in nine seconds could go from that paragraph to a bus turning head and stand there, and nobody would have to spend eleven seconds. THE PRICE ALREADY DID IT. THE FINDING, WHICH RECASTS 13 MARCH WITHOUT RESTATING IT: FINDABILITY IS NOT A THING THIS BOROUGH HAS BEEN MINTING. FINDABILITY IS WHAT A PRICE DOES WHEN THERE IS NO COUNTER IN FRONT OF IT. A counter is the cheapest privacy device anybody in this city has ever built and it costs nothing and nobody designed it and there is no form for it — three feet of wood between a person and a person. The man at the forty-slot board has no wall, because he has to take the money, and he is the most findable man in the lowland and has been for twenty-six years and nobody did it. THE REFUSAL, said out loud in a shop on the Friday, given to Beatrice Osunde, who did not argue and did not improve it: HE DID NOT GO TO THE BUS TURNING HEAD. The finder is the handle. The cheapest handle in this city is not a date. It is a person walking up to a pillar and being handed a bucket. THE MOST EXPENSIVE THING ANYBODY CAN DO TO A MAN WHO ALREADY HAS ONE IS TO GO AND INTRODUCE HIMSELF. He did not go on the Wednesday, the Thursday or the Friday, and nothing in that week said a man had chosen anything. A priced-access case is not a recognition case. Bea, once, about a list: a man who is visible because water costs money and a man who is visible because a woman in a hall said a shape out loud are not the same shape and are not the same man, and if anyone ever writes a sentence that puts them in the same list then the list is a list of people somebody can ring. On the Friday afternoon he counted the people through the door once, between about ten past three and about half past three, on the back of his left hand, and then he stopped, deliberately, and the reason he stopped is the finding: a count of people through a door is a level of service the moment anybody can be held to it. THE HOOK, STATED OUT LOUD: SOMEBODY WHO ASKS A QUESTION ABOUT A DAY HAS ALREADY DECIDED THAT A DATE IS WORTH HAVING, and that decision takes somebody about four months and a trough, and it is not something a counter can hand out, and if the counter handed it out it would be a service and it would scale and it would be a thing you could buy. SOMEBODY WHO DOES NOT ASK IS NOT REFUSING ANYTHING. HE HAS A CAN, OR A DRUM, OR A BAG, OR A ROUTE, AND HE IS IN A HURRY. A contradiction requires a second visit, a second visit is a decision, and nobody in this borough is required to decide anything at all. Nobody was helped, nobody was hurt, the water still costs what it costs, and the man is still not findable from any record in this city.**

**Chapter 0133 — What A Man Of Thirty-Eight Did On A Sunday. Wednesday 25 March 2015, about two in the afternoon, a kitchen on the top floor of a building on a road he has never seen the name of on any paper; about half past five the same evening, a corridor, ground floor, the Old Ward; and Friday 27 March, the same corridor. ONE QUESTION OF HIS IN THE WHOLE BATCH, six words, in a corridor, and a corridor is a corridor and he knew that when he asked it: `And who else has got it.` CLOSE POV PERMITTED AND USED ON EMMANUEL OTIENO, 38, who is not on the board and is not paid and is not the ninth seat. One window over a yard, a table with two chairs, a coat over one of them, a plate in the sink with the pattern worn through in the middle, a daughter of nine at school until half past three who comes in on the left foot first and puts her shoes on wrong and does them on the pavement if they are not by the door. He put them by the door. The first half of what he worked out: a person can ask a question here and get a date, and a date can be wrong, and if it is wrong you can come back. The second half: HE CAN NOW DO IT TO ANYTHING WITH A DATE ON IT. HE GAVE IT AWAY THREE TIMES AND WROTE NOTHING DOWN AND TOLD NOBODY: a woman of about thirty-one at the depot who asked him on a Tuesday in February how he knew a thing was true on a day and wrote nothing down either; a man of about fifty-five on the second floor of his building who asked him in that kitchen on a Sunday in March why he was so calm about a landlord; and a man about thirty he met once in a queue outside a hospital over a broken coffee machine, who had been at the same school in a different decade, and who had written a date on the back of his hand and asked him whether that counted. It counted. And the far end of it, worked out alone at that sink in February with the tap running, and told nobody: in about four years somebody is going to write a form, it will be a good form, and the woman who hands it over will not have chosen to be in it and will not be able to be un-in it, and the wall is not the counter, the wall is the day somebody can say *we can only do eight today* and be entirely correct, and it will not be the man who said it, it will never be the man who said it, it will be the first person who is tired and honest. Then the corridor, and a man of about thirty-four who works in a canteen and is on no list: `It's not strange any more, that's all I wanted.` And about eleven people at one counter, and one of them works somewhere with a name on it — a name on the drawing layer with a filled square beside it and an expiry and a node number, because eight of the nine shapes on that layer have names and the ninth has not got one and never will. **IT IS THE FIRST TIME IN NINE MONTHS THAT ANYTHING HAS COME OUT OF THAT COUNTER AND TOUCHED A DISTRICT, and he does not know which one and is not going to find out, and asking is how a discovery becomes a finding and a finding goes in a report and a report is a thing a person can be held to.** IT TRAVELS SIDEWAYS AND NOT UPWARDS — a woman on a shift, a man on a second floor, a man in a queue, a girl on a till, a kitchen on the other side of this borough — and every step of it is a person telling a person at a kettle and not one of the steps is a document. THE FEAR WAS THE WRONG FEAR: he had been afraid of a form since 4 September and it is not going to happen; what has happened instead is that it stopped being a thing anybody is doing, and a discovery has become weather. And the reason he will not go and ask him, nine words: **I AM THE PERSON WHO MADE IT CHEAP.** Nobody was helped, nobody was harmed, nothing was written down, and the man of thirty-eight went to work at four o'clock and did not open the glovebox.**

**Chapter 0134 — A Woman Who Was Asked, Twice, And Did Not Come. Monday 30 March 2015, a hall in Tideglass; and Wednesday 1 April, the vestibule. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION in either room. There is no number in this borough for anybody to telephone, and it is not on a list and it is not going on one, and the reason is four words long: **NOBODY OWNS THAT NUMBER.** A trestle table against the wall with a drawer with no key on it and no key in the building, and about nine people in that hall on the Monday and none of them went near it, and the reason is not a rule and there is no rule. THE ATTRIBUTION, WHICH IS NOW FIXED BECAUSE TWO EARLIER DOCUMENTS GOT IT WRONG: JONAS MERCER DID NOT WRITE THE FOUR LINES. What he did was say a name out loud, on Wednesday the third of December 2014, at about twenty to seven in the evening, in a room with about eleven people in it, in about nine seconds, and the minute of that evening says that a name was stated and does not contain the name. **THE FOUR LINES AT THE TOP OF THAT SHEET OF A4 ARE THE MINUTE-TAKER'S**, in biro, written on Wednesday the twenty-first of January in one go, without stopping, on a second sheet that had nothing at the top of it, and underneath them, in a different hand, four lines that are not hers and are nobody else's, and nobody has said out loud in a room that those four lines came from the woman and nobody is going to. The other piece of paper is a letter in a handbag in a kitchen off a landing and nobody in this borough has been in that kitchen. A NAME SAID IN A ROOM CANNOT BE UN-SAID, and that is the only thing about it anybody in this borough has ever been able to rely on. Beatrice Osunde's rule of 24 December, kept every day since: the last page of that pad is his, do not lose it, do not show it to anybody, and there is no box on it anywhere and that is also the point. SHE WAS ASKED TWICE, IN DAYLIGHT, IN ROOMS, WITH PAPERS, AND SHE DID NOT COME EITHER TIME, and a body of nine seats cannot require a person to do anything, not a form, not a signature, not a telephone call, and not to attend a sitting it is not required to hold, and the seven people in those seats have not met since the middle of January. **NOBODY CAN EVEN MAKE ANYBODY TRY TO MAKE HER COME, because a body cannot require a person to do anything, including the thing that would require the person to be required to do anything first, and that is not a paradox anybody in this borough has solved and it is not going to be solved.** The printed extract from Committee Services went back into a tray in that hall on the Monday at about eleven o'clock and the putting back took a woman about four seconds, and the putting back is the only thing anybody in this borough has done about that person in four months, and her name is not on it and there is no name box on it and there is no instrument in this city by which anybody could put a name on a piece of paper that has no name box. **THE FINDING: A HOLE IN THE RECORD IS THE ONLY HONEST THING ANYBODY IN THIS BOROUGH HAS PRODUCED ABOUT A PERSON IN NINE MONTHS**, and everything else is a thing, and this is the only one that is an absence, and the absence is a person, and it is the only one nobody can use. **AND THE PRICE, EXACT: THE MOST EXPENSIVE THING ANYBODY IN THIS CITY HAS PRODUCED ABOUT A PERSON IN NINE MONTHS IS A PIECE OF A4 IN A DRAWER WITH NO KEY ON IT, and nobody is going to open it, and the price of that piece of paper is that a woman of thirty-four is a person this borough can say nothing about at all. Not nothing nearly. Nothing at all. AND THAT IS THE CORRECT OUTCOME, IT IS NOT A CONSOLATION, AND THERE IS NO ARGUMENT IN THIS BOROUGH IN WHICH IT COULD BE MADE BETTER BY ANYBODY DOING MORE.** HOOK: the ninth seat has not been told and is not going to be, and a woman of fifty-four has been findable at a shopfront since the fourth of July, and nobody has asked her whether she can afford that, and this borough has no instrument for asking, and the only instrument it has ever had is a form, and a person's answer to *can you afford to be found* does not fit in a box and if it did it would not be true.**

**Chapter 0135 — The Counter Is Asked About The Gate. Friday 3 April 2015, a ground-floor corridor, Saint Orra west; Sunday 5 April, about twenty past six in the morning, four hundred yards from that building; Monday 6 April, the vestibule. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION in either corridor and there was no question of anybody's. **NOBODY ASKED THE COUNTER ANYTHING, AND A COUNTER THAT ANSWERS ABOUT A GATE IS A SERVICE, and the sentence that says so was given out loud on the fourth of March, and it is the only rule in this borough that anybody has ever been able to enforce on anybody else, AND IT WORKS BY NOBODY ENFORCING IT.** He went in to look at one thing: the eleven o'clock to half past one row, empty, not shaded, and it does not say *vacant*, and it has been that way since the second of April a year ago and a person coming up that corridor has read an empty row every morning since and has not been asked about it. On it, Bram Osei's own handwriting: **B. OSEI. NO. / *no reason I can give* / *(no reason given)*** — he has asked twice that the second line not be looked at and nobody looked at it. Priya Ranganathan's card is in a plastic sleeve on the Friday and Saturday rows because she put it there. **AND AT ABOUT TWENTY PAST TEN, IN A CORRIDOR, IN DAYLIGHT, ON PURPOSE, AND HE WAS NOT GOING TO BE ASKED A QUESTION AND HAD NOT BEEN ASKED ONE, BRAM OSEI SAID THREE WORDS: `You have not been down.` Twenty-seven days after the seventh of March and twenty days after the fourteenth. It is true. It is in one porter's ear and it is worth nothing. AND A PORTER IS A PERSON WHO CAN TELL THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN A MAN WHO IS THERE AND A MAN WHO IS NOT, and that is the first time anybody has established that in this account, and it was established by a man noticing on his own.** He had about nine hours to stop it and he went through three ways in about nine minutes and all three were closed: (1) he could not ask a porter not to say it, because he had told one on 2 March that he would not ask him a question, and a request is a question with better manners, and there would then be a man in that corridor who weighs what he is asked against what he was told; (2) he could not explain it, because a paragraph in a corridor is a form, it is the first of a series, if a porter can be given an account of a man's absences then a porter can be asked for them, and a person who can be asked for an account of a Saturday night is a person on a board with one row on it — and the man giving the account would be the man who wrote it; (3) he could not be there, because a fourth time in a corridor is a rota with one name in it, and a rota is the exact object this borough has spent nine months refusing to be, and it has already cost four tonnes of stone and about eleven hundred and eighty pounds off somebody's schedule and it has bought nobody anything at all. **AND THE COST, WHICH IS THE WHOLE OF THE CHAPTER, AND IT WAS PAID IN ONE PLACE AND IN ONE CURRENCY: A CORRECT THING WAS GOING TO HAPPEN IN A BUILDING, AND HE WAS THE ONLY PERSON HE KNEW OF WHO COULD SEE IT HAPPENING, AND NOBODY WAS GOING TO BE ASKED ABOUT IT, AND IT WAS GOING TO BE CORRECT.** The gate went up at seven on Sunday the fifth of April because two people who are not on anything were in a room with four chairs at ten o'clock the night before, and that is the same answer it has given every Sunday since the twenty-fifth of January and it is correct, and there is a version of this week in which a man notices a thing that is correct and says nothing, and this is that week. At about twenty past six he was four hundred yards away on purpose, and the term in force until twenty to six was the tenth, read at 22:00 on Saturday 28 March, in force since 07:00 on Sunday 29 March, and the eleventh was read at ten o'clock on the Saturday night before and began at seven on the Sunday, so there was an hour between six and seven when no term was in force at all. **AND WHAT HE FOUND OUT STANDING IN THE MIDDLE OF IT WAS THIS: FROM FOUR HUNDRED YARDS YOU CANNOT TELL WHETHER A DOOR IS OPEN OR SHUT, and that is not a failure of eyesight and it is not a tragedy. IT IS THE CORRECT AMOUNT OF EVIDENCE.** On the Monday the standing was unchanged: the rota has still not been rebuilt, the row is still empty and still does not say *vacant*, Bram Osei's Thursday is still refused, eleven of them since the twenty-fourth of January and not one has a date on it, the next is Saturday the eleventh of April and the one after that is the eighteenth, and there is no date on any of the three. **AND THE ONLY THING ANYBODY IN THAT BOROUGH GOT OUT OF THE WHOLE OF IT WAS A SENTENCE ABOUT A ROTA WITH ONE NAME IN IT, in a man's own handwriting, on a board at the end of a corridor, that nobody has looked at.** The bench steriliser on level zero is still running, its own strip goes in a drawer in the room about forty times a day, the hand that puts it there has not been found either, the frost warning came off on 2 April, and there is nothing at all in this borough that is going to happen in April.**

**Chapter 0136 — A Reason, Given To Somebody With A Pen. Wednesday 8 April 2015, the vestibule, ground floor, the Old Ward; and Friday 10 April, the records office, second floor. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION in either room, and nobody asked her anything about it afterwards, and he did not ask her whether she was going to put it in the minute, and he has not asked her anything since the twenty-fifth of February. **THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS THE MINUTES IS THE SAME PERSON WHO STANDS AT THAT COUNTER, AND JONAS MERCER TOOK TEN WEEKS TO WORK THAT OUT BECAUSE HE HAD BEEN ASSUMING THERE WAS A BOX SOMEWHERE THAT SAID SO, AND THERE IS NO BOX.** There is a sheet that says *minute taken by* and a name in it and that is the only name-box on the whole of the ground floor. The sheet is the minute until about four o'clock and after that it is only a sheet, and she is the only person in this city who can end a minute by picking one up and putting it down. If there were a box that tied *minute taken by* to *counter, ground floor* that would be a rota with one name in it, and the name would be hers, and the rota would be the establishment of a service floor in a building, by a person, in about eleven seconds. She is not on the board, not on a rota, is about fifty, has nineteen years in that office, and comes down at about ten and about three because nobody ever told her to and nobody could. ON THE WEDNESDAY, IN DAYLIGHT, IN A ROOM WITH ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE IN IT, SHE SAID A SENTENCE ABOUT HERSELF AND NOBODY ASKED HER AND IT TOOK ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS: **I DECIDE IT, AND THERE IS NOBODY I AM ALLOWED TO ASK WHETHER I HAVE DECIDED IT RIGHT, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF IT, AND I HAVE HAD THAT FOR NINETEEN YEARS.** **AND WHAT SHE HAD DONE WAS NOT SAY IT TO HIM. SHE SAID IT TO THE ROOM.** A sentence said to one person in a corridor is a thing two people know and can be used and belongs to the two of them — a porter said that to a man with a trolley on 2 March and was exactly right, and a man in a canteen in March said the other side of it, that a thing said in a room is a thing a room knows. **THERE IS NO BOX ON A ROOM.** No form in this borough has a room on it, no return, no field, no reference, no index and no rota, and a vestibule eleven feet by nine full of people on a Wednesday morning in April is not an establishment and cannot be interrogated. She chose the room because a room cannot be asked what happened in it and a corridor can, and a corridor is where a man asks a porter a question. A person who has been refusing things for nineteen years has had nobody ask her for a reason, and a person who has never been asked for a reason does not know whether her reasons are any good, and the only way to find out whether a sentence is true is to say it out loud where it can be overheard. The other half, about nine seconds, and it is the reason: she said it to him and not to the wall because he has had a pen since the fourth of September and has been writing down why other people say no for nine months, and nobody in this city has ever once asked him for one of his, and she found out over the course of a morning that she had got one, and had never told anybody that she did not have one until he handed it to her by not asking. He wrote it down, in about four seconds, on the left-hand page of a pad, at the end of the counter, because there was no minute — the board was not sitting and is not required to. She saw him do it, put her pen down, which is the thing she does about four times a year, and said: `Nobody in this borough has ever written down one of mine. You are going to. And it is going in a book that goes in a shop at the end of a street and it is not going anywhere, and I know that, and I am letting it, and do not come back and tell me what you did with it.` **THAT SENTENCE IS NOW IN ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE AND IN NO DOCUMENT, and that is precisely the thing she refused on the second of January, and it has happened anyway, and it was not anybody's fault, and nobody in this borough did it to her, and she went into that room on purpose.** Four people in that room heard it, two of them looked at her, and one of them said `what was that` a good deal later, at the end of the corridor, to somebody else and was told it was nothing, and nothing was the correct answer and it was not a lie. There is no item about the eighth of April in any minute and there is not going to be one. **IT IS NOW IN ONE HAND IN A BOOK THAT GOES NOWHERE, WHICH IS THE FIRST TIME ANYTHING SHE HAS SAID HAS GONE ANYWHERE AT ALL, AND SHE KNEW THAT WHEN SHE LET HIM DO IT, AND SHE LET IT.** Nobody may open that pad and nobody may ask what is in it. M. Furneaux and Howard Bramble did not enter the chapter and may not come back a third time. ONE LATTICE NOTICE IN THE WHOLE BATCH, on the wall of that vestibule: a register of narrowings is an account of nodes that are narrowed / a node is not a person / a person may be affected by every entry on this board and is named on none of them / no person signs this sheet. It defines nothing, including *holder*, and it requires nothing. The drawer: no key, no list, one hand, no reference, no number, no crest, no form, no box and no name on anything in it including hers, and it is not a file, because there is no document and there never was one, and she keeps a copy of every state she has written and the state a man carries away in a pocket is the same state about a day and neither of the two pieces of paper is a record of the other, and there is no form in this borough on which anybody could write that these are not a series, and a box that says *not a series* is a series, and there are more of them in that drawer than there were in March, and about eleven people a day is a count of people and the two were never added. ON THE FRIDAY, at about half past eleven, he went up to the second floor and saw a tray with post on top of something in it, an envelope not on borough stock with a departmental reference in the correct typeface, and he did not move the post, did not lift the bundle, did not look at the reference, and does not know what is under it. **AND THE ONE FACT ABOUT THAT DESK THAT IS A FACT AND NOT A STORY: A PIECE OF PAPER UNDER TWO OTHER PIECES OF PAPER IS A PIECE OF PAPER THAT HAS STOPPED BEING DEALT WITH, and that is the oldest way in the world of saying so, and there is no instrument anywhere that could go and find a thing like that on a tray and ask what it is. A register with nineteen entries and a blank line under the nineteenth says that nothing was added. A tray with post on top of something says that something was asked for and not dealt with, and it says it in a way that cannot be answered, because answering it would mean taking the post off.** HOOK, WRITTEN DOWN IN A SHOP: SHE IS GOING TO BE PROVED RIGHT ABOUT THE REGISTER, AND IT IS GOING TO BE ABOUT THE SHELF AND NOT ABOUT THE BULB, and she does not know that yet, and Jonas Mercer does, and is not going to tell her, and the reason he wrote it down is that if he told her she would be the woman who was warned.**

**Chapter 0137 — The Hour Between Six And Seven. Saturday 11 April 2015, about ten past ten at night, four hundred yards from a building; Sunday 12 April, about twenty past six in the morning, a ground-floor corridor, Saint Orra west; Monday 13 April, the vestibule. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION anywhere in the chapter. There was another handover on the Saturday night and it is not this chapter's business and it is not going to be dated here and no name is going on it and nobody is going to be asked about it. A term is six days, read at ten o'clock on the Saturday night before the Sunday it starts on, gate at seven, expiry at six the following Sunday, and the Saturday nights are what are a week apart, which is why there is an hour on every Sunday morning between six and seven when no term is in force at all. He worked it out on the Saturday afternoon, standing up, and it had not taken him any time at all, and what he had worked out was about a place: FOUR HUNDRED YARDS IS NOT AN ANSWER. FOUR HUNDRED YARDS IS A PLACE, AND A PLACE A MAN GOES TO IS THE FIRST HALF OF A SCHEDULE, AND A SCHEDULE IS A ROTA WITH ONE NAME IN IT, and he has stood on that pavement five times since December, two of them at ten o'clock on a Saturday night. It had been safe on 3 April only because on 3 April it was a decision he had made on the Friday and it was the first time, and a piece of wet pavement is a piece of wet pavement however far off it is. So he went and stood in the corridor instead, at about twenty past six, and he has stood in a corridor on a Sunday morning exactly twice, and the first of them was at half past four on the fourth of January, which is the last hour and a half of a term, and the second is this one, in the hour after a term has stopped, and the first had not been planned for a week and the second one had, and that is the whole of the difference. At twenty past six on Sunday the twelfth no term was in force anywhere in this city, the corridor was empty, and THERE WAS A DOOR FOUR FEET TO HIS LEFT AND IT WAS SHUT AND IT WAS A DOOR AND THERE WAS NOTHING BEHIND IT THAT WAS IN FORCE. **HE DID NOT OPEN IT.** He had a claim to a door in this volume and he had spent it on 28 January, in a room, in daylight, in about four seconds, and it is the only thing he has ever spent in nine months, and he was not going to go back to a thing he has already spent because it would make the next four days easier. **AND WHAT AN HOUR IS: IT IS A FLOOR THAT IS NOT LEVEL, AND THERE IS NOBODY IN IT.** In that hour four hundred and twenty women are not on the far side of a paragraph number, because there is no paragraph and no term; the eleven o'clock to half past one row is not empty, because there is no rota and no row; the register has no nineteenth entry with a bulb out on it. A term is a promise made by two tired people at ten o'clock on a Saturday night, and a promise that runs six days and then stops, made by two people who are not on anything, on their own time, with nothing written down, **IS THE CHEAPEST THING ANYBODY IN THIS CITY HAS EVER PRODUCED. And in about four years somebody is going to write a schedule for that hour, and the schedule will be good, and the day it goes up the hour stops being an hour and becomes a post.** THE FINDING: **THE HOUR CANNOT BE SCHEDULED WITHOUT SOMEBODY BEING REQUIRED TO BE IN IT.** Any schedule at all contains the sentence *somebody will be there*, and a sentence with somebody in it is a requirement, and a clause that cannot be challenged by a person cannot require them to, and a column with a name in it can be challenged by the person whose name is in it, and a person who is allowed to say no to a column is no longer a column, and then there is nothing on the wall at all. **SO THE ONLY WAY TO KEEP THE HOUR IS TO KEEP IT UNSCHEDULED, AND THE INSTRUMENT THAT KEEPS IT UNSCHEDULED IS THE SAME INSTRUMENT THAT HAS BEEN DOING EVERYTHING ELSE FOR NINE MONTHS, WHICH IS TWO PEOPLE WHO ARE NOT ON ANYTHING TURNING UP AT TEN O'CLOCK ON A SATURDAY BECAUSE NOBODY WROTE ANYTHING DOWN. It is the cheapest thing in this borough. It is the only thing that works. There is no instrument for it and there cannot be one and the attempt to make one is the exact moment this whole thing stops.** And then the arithmetic he had been avoiding: he has stood in that corridor on a Sunday morning exactly twice, the 4th of January at half past four was a decision, in the last hour and a half of a term and before there was an hour in the week with nothing in force, and this is the second, in the hour after a term has stopped, and he had arranged the whole Saturday to get here on purpose and there was nothing whatever wrong with any of it; a third is a habit and a fourth is a rota with one name in it, and there is no form on which anybody could stop him and none he would fill in. **THE HOUR DOES NOT NEED HIM. It has not needed him for four months. THE ONLY REASON HE WAS EVER THERE IS THAT HE WAS NOT REQUIRED TO BE ANYWHERE.** And the reason four hundred yards is not the answer is not the distance, and the reason standing in a corridor is not the answer is the same, and there is no third place, and the only instrument this borough has for a thing that must not become a rota is the absence of a rule, and an absence of a rule cannot be enforced on the one person who would break it, and that person is him. On the Monday nothing had moved: the rota, the empty row, Bram Osei's Thursday, the second line nobody looked at, Priya Ranganathan's card; the thirteenth of them is on Saturday the eighteenth of April and the fourteenth on the twenty-fifth, and there is a date on neither. **NOTHING WHATSOEVER HAPPENS ON WEDNESDAY 15 APRIL 2015 AND IT IS A WEDNESDAY.** The forty minutes is the time until a family knew, on the fifteenth of April of a year before this one, and it is a woman of eighty-four in bay six and it is not about a door and it is not about an hour and it is the only figure of minutes this borough has. In the shop, on the Monday evening, he said one thing: she had told him in December that a thing like that has to be said to somebody four times a day for a week before it holds, and he has said it to her once. And she said: `Four times a day for a week is for a thing you have to survive. Nobody survives standing in a corridor. Nobody has ever survived it. It is not a position. It is just a thing a man does until he is an old man who does it, and then it is a habit and then it is a rota, and there is no version of it where a man in his thirties has got a position at a bus turning head and a Sunday morning in it.` And: `I am not going to be asked twice, Mr Mercer, and I have told you that, and if you come in here on Wednesday with the same thing again in a different coat I shall put the broom in front of you.`**

**Chapter 0138 — What A Drift Looks Like From The Inside. Friday 17 April 2015, about two in the afternoon, a shop at the end of a street; Monday 20 April, from about ten past ten, the vestibule. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION in either room, and the man of about sixty asked four questions at that counter, the two shortest of them four words and one of them six words long, and not one of them was about anything. He had been doing arithmetic since the Friday morning and it was the fourth Friday running: EVERY WORD OF THE DRIFT IS THE OUTSIDE, and he had never once seen what it looks like from the side that has the paper. And he cannot see it, and that is the finding before the finding: a slip can only be produced by the person holding it, and the person holding it is not findable from any record in this city, and that is the design and not an accident, and he built half of it. On the Monday morning the man of about sixty came back and put a slip on the counter, flat, turned round so the writing was the right way up for her, which is what a person does with a thing they have been carrying for five weeks and have taken out about four times and have not known what to do with. `Is that a receipt.` He wanted to know whether it meant he owed somebody something, because if it did he would like to pay it and be rid of it. She did not touch it. **It is not a receipt. It is a state. It says what was true on a day, in a hand, and there is no name on it and there is no number on it and there is no form number and no box, and it was not given to you for any purpose that requires you to do anything, and you are not obliged to do anything with it, and you are not able to do anything with it.** And then the rest of it, about eleven seconds, and she has never said it in a room before: **YOU CANNOT PAY IT, BECAUSE THERE IS NOTHING TO PAY. YOU CANNOT RETURN IT, BECAUSE THERE IS NOBODY TO RETURN IT TO WHO IS REQUIRED TO RECEIVE IT. YOU CANNOT FILE IT, BECAUSE IT HAS NO REFERENCE AND THERE IS NO BOX ANYWHERE IN THIS BUILDING THAT WOULD TAKE IT. AND YOU CANNOT THROW IT AWAY, AND THAT ONE IS NOT ME. Throwing a piece of paper away is a decision about a document, and a person is supposed to be able to make that decision in about four seconds, and you cannot make this one in four seconds, and so it will be in your coat.** He said `Then I am sorry I asked` and she said nobody in this borough has ever said sorry to her at that counter and she would rather he did not make a thing of it. He put it back in the inside of his coat, where it had been, and that was the end of it. **THE INSIDE OF THE DRIFT: A STATE GIVEN ON A DAY STOPS BEING A STATE ABOUT A DAY AND BECOMES AN OBJECT IN A COAT, and the moment it becomes an object it needs a procedure, and there is no procedure on the sheet, and so it goes in a pocket. And nobody comes back to argue it, because a man with a piece of paper in a coat has no way of knowing whether he has been told something true, and the only way to know is to go back and ask, and going back is a decision, and nobody is required to make a decision. THAT IS THE DRIFT, AND IT IS NOT A THING THE COUNTER DOES. It is a thing that happens to a person who is handed a correct answer and given nothing to do with it.** And the shelf: a register that grows when nobody is hurt is a register somebody can hold over you, and a drawer of slips grows exactly the same way, out of correct things done by careful people, nobody hurt by it, nobody helped by it, growing at the rate of a person walking through a door and asking a question that somebody is not required to ask. **THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE REGISTER AND THE DRAWER IS ONE LINE LONG: NOBODY EVER WROTE A RULE FOR THE PERSON.** The register has a blank line and no rule about who may write under the nineteenth. The drawer has no line. Not a blank one. A person has no line at all. And the arithmetic of the two: nine slips is a count of documents and about eleven people a day is a count of people and they may never be added; the second drawer is a plastic wallet in the glovebox of a van he does not own and nobody in this borough can say how many pieces of paper are in it, and that is correct. **AND IN ABOUT FOUR YEARS SOMEBODY IS GOING TO BE ABLE TO SAY HOW MANY. AND HOW MANY IS A CAPACITY. A CAPACITY IS A PROMISE. THE DAY THERE IS A NUMBER OF WHAT A COUNTER DOES, THERE IS A MORNING SOMEBODY CAN SAY *WE CAN ONLY DO EIGHT TODAY* AND BE ENTIRELY CORRECT, AND THE CUTTING WILL NOT BE DONE BY ANYBODY CRUEL. IT WILL BE DONE BY A NUMBER. NOBODY HAS TO CUT ANYTHING. THE NUMBER DOES IT BY EXISTING.** And Bea Osunde, who does not read the pad: `In about four years it is not going to be you that it matters to, is it. It is going to be some poor devil of a frightened person in a kitchen, and somebody is going to put something on that table, and it is not going to be the bulb.` THE HOOK, AND IT IS NOW A STATED FINDING: **A SLIP IN A DRAWER IS GOING TO BE EVIDENCE ONE DAY. Not his evidence. A PIECE OF PAPER THAT SOMEBODY KEPT BECAUSE THEY COULD NOT THINK WHAT ELSE TO DO WITH IT, HANDED OVER BY A PERSON WHO THINKS THEY ARE HELPING.** And there is no version of that piece of paper in which the person it was written for is in it, because they were not, because they are never in it, because there is no name box and there is not going to be one, and nobody is going to be able to say afterwards whether it was a service or a choice, and neither is he, and that is the cost of it, and he has known the price of it since the twenty-sixth of January and has never written it down and is not going to.**

**Chapter 0139 — A Saturday Night, The Fourteenth. Friday 24 April 2015, the vestibule; about two in the afternoon, a shop at the end of a street; Sunday 26 April, about twenty past six in the morning, four hundred yards from a building. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION anywhere, and there was one question of a stranger's at that counter on the Friday morning, not about anything, and the counter did not answer it and the counter was right and he was four feet away for all of it. It is eleven feet by nine and there is no chair and a chair is not one of the three parts of a body's one power. A woman of about thirty came in behind him and asked: `Is the man who stands at the wall on a list.` He was four feet away with his back to the wall and did not move and did not say anything. She said she was not asking who he was, she was asking whether there was a piece of paper anywhere with his name on it, because a man at work told her a person comes in there and stands at the wall and does not ask for anything. **She was told no, in about four seconds, and the second half as well, which is that he is right and it does not help her, and there is no form on any counter in that building that would take her question, and if there were one then in about four years there would be a list of people who stand at walls, and she could not be given it and neither could anybody else, and she would rather she went away knowing that than knowing it was not there. `That's a shame. That's not an answer.` `It is the answer. It is about four seconds long and you did not like it and I did not enjoy it.` She went out into the street and did not come back. **AND HE HAD COME IN TO LOOK AT A BLANK LINE AND HAD BEEN STOOD AT BY A STRANGER ASKING A RECORDS COUNTER WHETHER A MAN AT A WALL IS WRITTEN DOWN SOMEWHERE, AND THE COUNTER WAS CORRECT, AND THE ANSWER COST ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND IT WAS CORRECT BECAUSE A COUNTER THAT ANSWERS ABOUT A PERSON IS A SERVICE, AND A SERVICE IS A ROTA WITH ONE NAME IN IT, AND HERS IS THE NAME IT WOULD HAVE. THIS IS THE FIRST TIME IT HAS COME BACK AT HIM: he has been standing at that wall since the twenty-eighth of January and a man in a canteen in March and a woman of thirty in April have both heard about it, and neither got it from him, and one of them got it from a man who does not talk about his Fridays. And the answer that came back was the correct one, and refusing does not make the person safer, it makes them unattacked. He has been living on that difference for four months and it is the only instrument in this borough that works, and IT DOES NOT PROTECT ANYBODY. IT ONLY MEANS NOBODY IS AIMING.** At about two o'clock he went into the shop with the pad and DID NOT SAY THE THING, and that is the first time in nine months he has come into that shop with a sentence in him and gone out without it, and the reason is not that he has changed his mind. THE REASON IS THAT SHE TOLD HIM IN DECEMBER THAT A THING LIKE THAT HAS TO BE SAID TO SOMEBODY FOUR TIMES A DAY FOR A WEEK BEFORE IT HOLDS, AND HE HAS SAID IT TO HER ONCE, ON THE SEVENTH OF APRIL, AND A THING THAT HAS BEEN SAID ONCE HAS NOT BEEN TESTED, AND A THING THAT HAS NOT BEEN TESTED IS A PREFERENCE. HE IS NOT GOING TO ASK HER AGAIN, HE IS NOT GOING TO ANYBODY ELSE WITH IT, AND NOBODY IS GOING TO BE TOLD. And she said: `The oil came in on Tuesday and the tin is by the door and I would like it moved before the weekend. And that is not the same conversation and I am telling you that it is not the same conversation so that you know I know it is not the same conversation.` **AND THE REASON HE HAS NOT GONE HAS CHANGED, AND THE CHANGE IS THE WHOLE OF THE CHAPTER. On the thirteenth of April the reason was about them — I must not become a rota with one name in it in a building full of tired people. ON THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF APRIL THE REASON IS ABOUT HIM: HE HAS BEEN DOING IT FOR NOTHING FOR FOUR MONTHS AND THE ENTIRE PRODUCT IS THAT TWO TIRED MEN COULD SAY THAT HE WAS THERE. That is all. And he has worked out that he would rather be that than a name on a list. AND THAT IS NOT A PRINCIPLE. IT IS A PRICE. And a price is a thing you pay and a reason is a thing you hold, and he has had this one since the thirteenth of April and it has not been tested, and it is the fourth thing in nine months he has not handed to anybody, and it is the first one he wanted.** And on the Saturday night there was a handover at ten o'clock four hundred yards from a piece of wet pavement, the fourteenth of them, and there is no document about it and there is not going to be one, and there has not been a date written down on any of the fourteen. Two men were in a waiting area with four chairs and a machine for hot water. **THEY HAVE READ EVERY ONE OF THESE ALOUD SINCE THE THIRD OF JANUARY 2015 AND NEITHER OF THEM HAS EVER BEEN ASKED ABOUT A SINGLE ONE OF THEM BY ANYBODY AND NEITHER EVER WILL BE, AND THE REASON IS NOT DISCRETION. THE REASON IS THAT THERE IS NOTHING TO ASK ABOUT.** And nobody in that building knows that a man was four hundred yards away on purpose, and a porter told a man in a corridor on 2 March that he would not say either thing, and he has told nobody, and there has been nothing since that required him to. At about twenty past six on Sunday the twenty-sixth he was four hundred yards away. The term that had been in force was the thirteenth, begun at seven o'clock on Sunday the nineteenth, six days, stopped at six that morning. **THE HOUR BETWEEN SIX AND SEVEN ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF APRIL WAS THE HOUR IN WHICH NO TERM WAS IN FORCE AT ALL, AND IT WAS THE FOURTH OF THOSE IN FOUR WEEKS, AND THERE IS NO DATE IN THIS CITY FOR IT, AND THERE WAS ONE ON THE FIFTH OF APRIL AND ONE ON THE TWELFTH AND ONE ON THE NINETEENTH, AND NOBODY WROTE ANY OF THEM DOWN AND NOBODY IS GOING TO.** At seven o'clock the fourteenth term came in, read at ten o'clock the night before, six days, expiring at six o'clock on Sunday the third of May. **THERE IS NO CROSSOVER. THERE IS NO HOUR IN WHICH TWO OF THEM ARE RUNNING AND THERE IS NO HOUR IN WHICH ONE OF THEM RUNS AN HOUR PAST. THERE IS AN HOUR EVERY WEEK IN WHICH THIS CITY HAS NOTHING, AND THEN THERE IS A DOOR.** And a light came on in a car park he could not see into and it was nothing, and it was the eleventh of those since the twenty-fifth of January, which is ten weeks and four days, and nobody in this borough is entitled to say he had noticed it. And then the whole of the standing: the rota, the empty row, Bram Osei's Thursday and the second line nobody looked at, the fifteenth on Saturday the second of May with no date on it, the register at nineteen with the blank line blank for a hundred and forty-nine days, the card in the slot, the tray with post on top of something in it, a drawer with no key, about eleven people a day, two conditions sixteen inches apart. **AND A WOMAN IN THIS BOROUGH WHO IS GOING TO BE ASKED SOMETHING IN APRIL, OR IN MAY, OR IN JUNE, AND IT IS NOT GOING TO BE JONAS MERCER, AND HE IS NOT GOING TO STOP IT, AND HE IS NOT GOING TO BE IN THE ROOM.** She is fifty-four and has stood in a shopfront since the fourth of July without being paid a penny and she is the only door in a building of thirty-one rented rooms, and nobody has told her what that is and nobody is going to. On the eleventh of December, in a kitchen, she said she has not got an answer ready and is not going to get one ready in a room, and that it is the same refusal as the day after and the same reason and it stands. **AND NOBODY IN THIS BOROUGH IS ENTITLED TO MAKE HER GET ONE READY, and the only thing anybody in this borough can do about it is the thing he has just spent a fortnight deciding to do with a Sunday morning, which is to be somewhere he is not required to be and to let the correct thing happen without him.**

**Chapter 0140 — What A Service Floor Is, In April. Monday 27 April 2015, the vestibule, ground floor, the Old Ward, and Wednesday 29 April, the same room. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION in either room. He was asked a question in it on the Monday morning, in daylight, by somebody who is not on the agenda either, and it was the third question anybody has asked him, and he answered it. **IT IS ELEVEN FEET BY NINE AND THERE IS NO CHAIR AND THERE WAS NO CHAIR AND THERE IS NOT GOING TO BE ONE, AND A BODY OF NINE SEATS HAS ONE POWER, WHICH IS TO REQUIRE A PARTY HOLDING CONTINUITY TO ANSWER, RECEIVE THE ANSWER, AND BE ASKED TO CONSIDER A MATTER, AND A CHAIR IS NOT ONE OF THE THREE PARTS OF IT. HERE IS WHO WAS IN THAT ROOM, AND THIS IS NOT A LIST AND IT IS NOT A ROLL-CALL AND NOBODY IN THAT ROOM WAS CALLED ANYTHING, AND NOBODY IN IT WAS ASKED ANYTHING: the man of about fifty-six, who is not a member, did not come as a member, and is not on any list, and who has asked the only three questions anybody has asked; the woman who keeps the minutes, not named in this account and not going to be, whose name is in a box on the sheet that says *minute taken by*, and there is no other box anywhere in this borough that says who wrote a thing down; a woman of about sixty who has been in that corridor every morning for about a year reading a board with an empty row on it and has never once been asked about it; and eight other people who are on no board, who have not been asked anything and are not going to be.** THE RECORDS SUPERVISOR OF ABOUT FIFTY AND THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS THE MINUTES ARE ONE PERSON AND NOT TWO, and there is one of her in this account and she is the woman at that counter, and no box anywhere in this borough could say so. AND HERE IS WHO WAS NOT IN THAT ROOM, AND NONE OF THEM WAS LEFT OUT, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM WAS LEFT OUT ON PURPOSE BY SOMEBODY STANDING AT A WALL. The ninth seat, fifty-four, unpaid, findable at a shopfront since 4 July, not told. The man of forty-four, who could not have been asked to come. The man of forty-nine, who could be asked one question tomorrow morning and has not been. The man of forty-eight, not asked, not going to be. A woman of thirty-four, in about eleven people and in no document at all. A woman of seventy-two, behind a door, a great deal more frightened and no more findable. The four hundred and twentieth woman, with no person on the near side of that paragraph.
