A man of thirty-eight, who worked out a thing on his own in a kitchen in February and gave it away to three people and told nobody, and one of those three works somewhere in this borough that has a name, and Jonas Mercer does not know which one and is not going to find out and has not written it down. AT ABOUT HALF PAST TEN THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SIX ASKED IT AND DID NOT APOLOGISE AND DID NOT SAY *MAY I*, AS ON EITHER OF THE OTHER TWO OCCASIONS: `What is it for.` **AND JONAS MERCER DID NOT ANSWER THAT, AND SAID SO, AND IT TOOK ABOUT FOUR SECONDS: that is a question about a thing, he is going to turn it round, and it will still be a question, and then he is going to answer it, and it will be the shortest thing he has ever said in a room, and he is not going to improve it afterwards, and he is not going to say it again this year. `Turn it round.` `Who is it for.` `That is the same question.` `No. It is not. It is the other one. And I have answered the one you asked, twice, out loud, in daylight, in this room, and I am not going to say either of them a third time, and neither of them is going to get better the third time and I have found that out the hard way over nine months.`** And then he said it, one sentence, not a scheme, not the five items, not what anything costs, not on a wall and not going on one: **IT IS FOR THE PEOPLE THE BOROUGH CANNOT COUNT.** Nobody said anything for about four seconds. The man of about fifty-six did not ask a second question; he has asked three questions of anybody in this borough in nine months and got three answers and he did not ask a fourth. Nobody repeated it, nobody improved it, nobody has put it on a wall, the only place it has ever been is the fourth item of a sheet that requires nothing, the woman who keeps the minutes did not ask him whether he wanted it read back and he did not ask her to, and he still has five words in biro on the back of his left hand that nobody has ever asked him about. **AND THE MINUTE: Item 4, a person present, not on the agenda, not a party, not a member and not a departmental officer, was asked a question in the room, in daylight, by a person who is also not on the agenda; he declined the question as asked, put another question in place of it, and answered that one. Recorded: that the purpose of this floor is people this borough cannot count. Recorded: that no person so described was in the room, that none of them has been asked whether they can afford it, that this body has no instrument for asking, and that the reason is that this body cannot require any person to answer anything, including that. Recorded: that this is the third answer given in this room in nine months and that none of the three has been repeated and that none of the three has been placed on a wall. Recorded: that this minute has no box for the name of a person, and that the sheet is the minute until about four o'clock and after that it is only a sheet. Nothing in this item requires any person to do anything.** AND WHAT IT IS NOT: NAMING A PURPOSE IS NOT PRODUCING ONE. A floor that is for the people the borough cannot count is not a floor that has counted them, and does not seat them, and does not put them in a register, and does not require anybody to do anything, and cannot. The lowland is still not on the layer, is still not in the eight districts, still cannot be seated, and the reason is still that a node with no party is not a party and a place is not a person. **AND A PERSON WHO IS NOT COUNTED IS NOT A PERSON WHO IS HELPED. THAT SENTENCE HAS BEEN TRUE SINCE THE SECOND OF JUNE AND IT HAS NOT BEEN REFUTED AND IT IS NOT REFUTED BY NAMING A PURPOSE, AND SAYING OUT LOUD WHAT A FLOOR IS FOR HAS NOT MADE ONE SINGLE PERSON IN THIS CITY ANY CLOSER TO BEING HELPED, AND EVERYBODY IN THAT ROOM KNOWS IT, AND THAT IS THE CORRECT OUTCOME AND IT IS NOT A VICTORY.** Then the whole standing of the borough on Wednesday 29 April at about twenty to eleven, in full: the counter and the eleven seconds; nineteen entries and a hundred and fifty-four days and a bulb; the card in the slot for sixty-six days; the drawer and the two numbers that were never added; the tray and post on top of something for sixty-eight days; two conditions sixteen inches apart; paragraph 4.11 unanswered in front of four hundred and twenty women and the strip going in a drawer about forty times a day by a hand that has not been found; seven of nine and no chair; a method that has travelled sideways and touched a district and is in no document; a man of forty-four with a bucket and nobody able to ask him about a coat; a man of forty-nine who can be asked tomorrow and has not been; a man of forty-eight who is not going to be asked; a man of thirty-eight who is findable now and does not know it; a woman of thirty-four in about eleven people and in no document and a hole in the record as the only honest thing anybody in this borough has produced about a person in nine months; a woman of fifty-four findable at a shopfront and not told; a woman of seventy-two behind a door; **a woman of sixty-one with nine hundred and eighty-six Wednesdays on a card under a rubber band in a biscuit tin, a number of Wednesdays and not a number of people, and nobody has ever asked her about it**; the gate going up at seven on a Sunday because two people who are not on anything were in a room with four chairs at ten o'clock on the Saturday night, fourteen of those Saturday nights reached and not one with a date on it, the term in force being the fourteenth and expiring at six o'clock on Sunday the third of May; the claims box closed and empty; the sealed envelope; the Weir; Prue Calloway's finding, opened once, not resolved, not going to be opened again, of which only the consequence is left and nobody has been made to live under it yet; and Jonas Mercer, who is not a member of anything and may not chair anything and has been all three of gallery, corridor and not in the room, and is not going to be any of the other things. **AND THE QUESTION THAT IS LEFT, WHICH IS NOT HIS AND WHICH NOBODY IN THIS BOROUGH IS ENTITLED TO ANSWER IN APRIL: A FLOOR IS FOR THE PEOPLE THE BOROUGH CANNOT COUNT. THERE IS A CARD IN A SLOT. THERE IS A LETTER IN A TRAY. THERE IS A REGISTER WITH A BLANK LINE UNDER IT THAT IS THE ONLY FIXED POINT IN THE BUILDING AND IT HAS BEEN BLANK A HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FOUR DAYS. THERE IS A SATURDAY NIGHT EVERY WEEK WITH NO DATE ON IT AND TWO TIRED MEN WHO READ IT ALOUD AND NEITHER OF WHOM HAS EVER BEEN ASKED. AND ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE A DAY WALK UNDER TWO CONDITIONS THAT DO NOT AGREE, AND GET A DATE, AND GO OUT AGAIN. SO THE COUNTING IS THE THING THAT IS MISSING, AND THE COUNTING IS NOT A JOB ANYBODY CAN BE GIVEN, AND NOBODY IN THIS CITY MAY BE REQUIRED EVEN TO BE TOLD, AND A FORM HAS A BOX ON IT, AND THE BOX IS WHERE THE NAME WOULD GO, AND THE NAME IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT HAS BEEN REFUSED FOR NINE MONTHS AND IT IS THE ONLY THING ANYBODY IN THIS BOROUGH HAS EVER ACTUALLY NEEDED. AND NOBODY HAS WRITTEN ANY OF IT DOWN. VOLUME 03 IS NOT CLOSED. CHAPTER 0140 IS NOT THE VOLUME'S CLOSE AND SAYS SO.**

**Chapter 0141 — What Becomes Of A Thursday. Thursday the thirtieth of April 2015, from about ten past ten in the morning, the vestibule, ground floor, the Old Ward; and about half past eleven, the trough by the lock-ups. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION in either place and nobody asks him anything either. THERE WERE FOUR QUESTIONS OF A STRANGER'S AT THAT COUNTER AT ABOUT A QUARTER PAST ELEVEN, THE SHORTEST OF THEM TWO WORDS, NOT ONE OF THEM ABOUT ANYTHING, THE COUNTER ANSWERED NONE OF THE FOUR, THE COUNTER WAS RIGHT ABOUT ALL FOUR, AND NOBODY HAS EVER BEEN TOLD NO AT THAT COUNTER ABOUT A PIECE OF PAPER BEFORE AND NOBODY HAD UNDERSTOOD THE WHY AT THE SAME TIME AS THE NO UNTIL THAT MORNING. THE ARITHMETIC HE CAME FOR, FOUR ITEMS, NO TOTAL: FOUR PIECES OF PAPER IN THIS BOROUGH MENTION THE TROUGH BY THE LOCK-UPS. The oldest is dated 26 January and mentions it in a line between four others and went into the inside pocket of a man with a bowser in the rain and has not been looked at once. The second is dated the second of February and mentions nothing else. The third is dated the thirteenth of February and mentions nothing else. ALL THREE OF THOSE ARE IN THE SAME INSIDE POCKET IN THE SAME COAT, and the man has not stood in that room since the middle of February, and there is no reason he would come back, and he is right not to come back. The fourth is dated the sixteenth of March, is about the second of February, and is in the inside pocket of a man with a galvanised can who has never been in that vestibule in his life and who came once more on the twentieth of April to ask whether a piece of paper was a receipt, was told no, put it back in the coat where it had been, and has not been back. **SO A SINGLE TROUGH, ON A SINGLE MORNING IN FEBRUARY, IS THE SUBJECT OF FOUR PIECES OF PAPER IN TWO INSIDE POCKETS, every one of them true, and not one of them producible by anybody, and there is no way in this city to ask which of them is the state of the trough, because a state is a person's account of a thing at ten past ten on a day, and there are two men, and they are both right, and neither of them may be required to answer anything, including a correction.** AND THE ROUTINE: a piece of paper in an inside pocket has become a thing with a routine; a routine with one man in it is a rota; nobody wrote the rota. He worked out in April, in a corridor, with a porter, that he could not stop it, and went through three ways and all three were closed — he cannot ask the man to stop coming, because a man may not be required to answer anything; he cannot put it in writing, because a paragraph in a corridor is a form and the man who gives it is the man who wrote it; and a man who is regularly four hundred yards away is a man who is regularly four hundred yards away. **Nobody in that room knows what day anybody comes, and he is not going to tell them, and he is not going to be asked.** THE VAN MAN: a man of about forty-five, forty, never been in that vestibule in his life, in a driver's coat, with a cardboard wallet and grease on the side of the second finger of his right hand, opens the wallet and there are THREE pieces of paper in it, about four inches by two, folded once, with a date on each and a hand on each and nothing else on any of them — no reference, no number, no crest, no form, no box, and no name. `Are these yours.` `Say the rest of it.` `Are those three pieces of paper yours. That is all I am asking and then I will go.` `I have never seen them before this morning, and I could not tell you whose they are if I had, and there is nothing on them to tell me, and I am not going to guess and I am not going to be asked to.` `That is not an answer.` `It is the whole of the answer I have got, and I am going to give you the rest of it and then you will go, because I have not got time and you have not got a bus to catch that I know of.` **AND WHAT SHE SAID THEN, ELEVEN SECONDS, IS THE CORRECT ANSWER AND THE ONLY THING SHE IS ENTITLED TO DO WITH A PIECE OF PAPER IN THAT ROOM, AND SHE HAS BEEN DOING THIS FOR NINETEEN YEARS: she cannot tell him whose they are, she could not if she wanted to because she has got no way of knowing and she is not going to work it out and there is nothing on them to work it out from, because there is no name on them and no number and no box, and no box is going to be on them. `Right. So there is a list.` There is no list; there is not a list of them and there is not a list of people, and she is not able to write one, and there is nowhere in this borough to write one, and that is not an oversight, and if there were somewhere to write one then in about four years somebody would be able to look one up, and the first thing anybody would do with a list like that is find out whether a person is on it, and the second thing anybody would do is ask that person how they feel about being on it, and neither of those has anything to do with her or with him or with three pieces of paper in a wallet. `So I have to keep them.` Nobody is going to make him keep them and nobody is going to make him throw them away either, and those are the same sentence. He understood it in about four seconds and it is the only time in nine months that Jonas Mercer has watched a person at that counter be told no and be told why at the same time and come out holding a different idea of what is on the other side of the counter. `So they are not mine.` They are not his, and they are not not-his, and that is the half she cannot help him with. He has had them since about Christmas. He put them back in the inside of his coat without hurrying and went out. **AND THE FINDING: THE THREE PIECES OF PAPER IN THAT VAN HAVE NO OWNER AND NO PERSON ATTACHED TO THEM, and the oldest of them is about three months old, and the two people they came from are not going to be asked, and the man carrying them is not going to be able to say whose they are, and there is nothing in this city that can find out, and in about four years somebody is going to hand a piece of paper to a frightened person in a kitchen and it is not going to have a bulb on it. THE ANSWER TO WHAT THAT IS GOING TO BECOME IS NOTHING, BECAUSE THERE IS NO INSTRUMENT, AND THAT IS CORRECT, and it is the answer the trough gave him in January and the answer the corridor gave him in February, and he has been given it four times and has not improved it once.** THE TROUGH, AT ABOUT HALF PAST ELEVEN, AND HE WROTE NOTHING DOWN AND THE REASON IS THREE SENTENCES LONG: it is not frozen and has not been since the Friday night before the second of February, and there is something in the bottom of it that is not water and is not what was in it in February. **A THING CAN BE LOOKED AT AGAIN — a thing is out there and anybody may look at it, and that is the closest this borough gets to a correction, and it has no form in it, and it costs nothing, and it binds nobody, and there is no instrument anywhere that could go and record that a man stood over a trough on a Thursday morning and saw a thing that was not there in February.** He could have written a state and he did not: if he had, and it had not agreed with the first two, there would be a contradiction, and a contradiction can only be moved by going back to the men, and the men may not be required to answer anything, including a correction — so the third piece of paper would have been a thing in this borough that could never be moved by anybody, ever, and he would have been the one who put it there. And the only thing he is able to do about a wrong statement in this city is look at the thing it is about, and he did it, and there is no way on earth of writing down that he did. The day's standing: the blank line a hundred and fifty-five days, the card in the slot sixty-nine days, the letter in the tray seventy-one days, all eleven standing refusals standing and not a twelfth, the two conditions sixteen inches apart and not reconciled, and about eleven people a day going in and out of a room eleven feet by nine and getting a date. Nobody was harmed, nobody was helped, nothing was resolved, and no piece of paper was written on that Thursday morning by anybody.

**Chapter 0142 — Asked On A Landing. Friday the first of May 2015, from about ten past ten in the morning, the vestibule, ground floor, the Old Ward; and about a quarter to eleven, a landing, a block of flats; and the Tuesday and the Thursday after that. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION in any of the three places and nobody asks him anything either. THERE WAS A QUESTION ASKED ON THAT LANDING ON THE FRIDAY MORNING, BY A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY, TO A WOMAN WHO IS NOT IN ANY LIST IN THIS BOROUGH AND NEVER HAS BEEN, it was about eight words long, it was not about anything, and the person who would have known whether there was anybody in that building to give a card to was not there and is not going to be told. IN THE VESTIBULE, which is a Friday and he is in that room on Fridays: the board read nineteen entries and a blank line under the nineteenth, a hundred and fifty-six days, and a woman of about fifty came down at about ten with a tray, and neither of them said one word to the other. **AND HE WORKED OUT, STANDING THERE, THE THING THAT ROOM HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED AND THAT HE WAS NOT GOING TO ASK IT EITHER: THE ENTIRE MECHANISM RUNS ON PEOPLE BEING IN THE SAME PLACE AT THE SAME HOUR EVERY MORNING. A counter, a board, a slot for post, a tray upstairs, a drawer with no key — none of it works because anybody built it, and all of it works because the same faces come past it in the same order on the same day. He has stood at that wall on Fridays since the twenty-eighth of January and has never once asked who else comes at ten, and he is not going to now, and if he asked it would be the first question anybody in nine months had asked in there about a person and it would be about all of them at once, and a question about all of them at once is a register.** THE WOMAN: she is about sixty, nobody in this account knows her name, and she has been in that corridor every morning since about the beginning of last summer, which is about a year, and has never once been asked about it. The reason she goes is the mornings; the flat is warm and there is a bus from the top of that street and the corridor at Saint Orra west is on the way, and the board at the end of it is about nine feet long and about four feet off the floor. The eleven o'clock to half past one row is empty, is not shaded, and does not say *vacant*. Further down there is a Thursday in a porter's own handwriting that says NO and a second line under it in somebody else's hand that says nothing at all, and a card in a plastic sleeve on the Friday row and the Saturday row because somebody put it there herself, which she thinks of as the same sort of thing as putting your own coat on a hook. **She does not know what any of it means and she has never said a word about it to anybody, and there is nobody in this borough she could say it to who would be allowed to do anything about it afterwards, and that is not a comfort and she has not been treating it as one. It is just a board that has been there longer than she has.** THE LANDING, AT A QUARTER TO ELEVEN: a young man of about thirty with a plastic folder under his arm and a card in his hand. `Are you the one who does the board.` Not said unkindly; said the way a person says a thing when they have been told something and are checking it. `No.` `Right.` He said he had been told to leave this somewhere in the building, that there was a man coming Monday to take a floor up and he had to be honest about the noise, and that his mate had said ask at the clinic because he had said there was a woman in that corridor every morning who would know who to give it to — and that he did not say what her name was, he just said there was a woman. `There is a woman. I am not her.` `Right. Sorry.` **AND HERE IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT HAPPENED, AND IT TOOK ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND IT IS CORRECT BECAUSE OF A RULE PUT IN FRONT OF A ROOM IN THIS CITY IN JUNE AND NEVER ARGUED OUT BY ANYBODY: she goes and reads the board and that is all she does, and she could not tell him what is on it and is not going to start; and she cannot give him anybody to give it to, because she is not anything, and there is no list anywhere that says she is anything, and there is nowhere in this borough she could be put on one, and that is not an oversight either — because if somebody had asked her in April whether she was the woman who did that board, and had written down that she said no, then she would be a person who had been asked about a job, and a person who has been asked about a job is on something, and you cannot get off it again, and she is not going to be on something because a man on a landing handed her a card. `I only wanted somebody to give it to.` `I know. And I cannot give it to anybody, because I am not anything.` He asked whether she knew who was on in the mornings. `No. I don't know that either.` `Right.` He stood there a second, said sorry, and went down the stairs with the card still in his hand, and did not leave it on the mat of the flat on the left and did not put it under the door of the flat on the right, **and nobody in that building was given a card about a floor being taken up on the Monday.** AND THERE WAS A SECOND PERSON ON THAT LANDING AND SHE SAW THE WHOLE OF IT: a woman going down to the shops with a bag over her wrist came out of her door at the moment he said *are you the one who does the board*, and she stopped, and said nothing, and went on down the stairs, and has not said anything to anybody. **That is all being seen to be asked is. It goes nowhere, and it is the only record of it, and there is not going to be another one.** THE FINDING, AND THE PRICE: **The whole of what a price does is that somebody else pays it. NOT THE MAN WHO ASKED. He came to a building on a Friday morning with a card and a folder and was told the truth in about four seconds by a woman who is nobody, and he was not angry and did not go away feeling clever, and carried the card back into his van in the same pocket, and he has not thought about it since, and he is going to be all right. And the person left holding it is a woman of about sixty who goes out at ten in the morning because the mornings are the only part of the day she goes out, and who was seen to be asked, and who cannot be released from it, and the reason she cannot be released from it is the reason everything in this borough cannot be released from anything: NOBODY MAY BE REQUIRED TO BE TOLD ANYTHING, and a person who is asked is a person who is on something, and there is no form in this city on which a person could be recorded as not being on something, because a form with a name on it is a form that has asked.** The only way to stop being asked about the door is to stop being at the door, and the door is the only part of her day she has got, and nobody is entitled to ask her about the mornings, and she is not going to explain them. ON THE TUESDAY a man at the bins asked her whether she knew who lived at number four B. ON THE THURSDAY a girl on the second landing asked her whether the water was going off at the weekend. Both about thirty seconds long, both ordinary, she said no to both, and was not thanked for it, and was not helped with either, and afterwards stood at her own window for a bit. **The rest of the week was a week. The problem is that a thing happened at half past ten on Friday in a corridor and there is no part of her, now, that is the same as it was at a quarter to eleven. Nothing was taken from her, nobody told her anything, a man asked her a question and she gave him the correct answer and he went away with his card. And the whole of what she has instead is that in a building of about thirty flats a person has been established as the one who is asked, and she did not agree to it, and she cannot be released from it, and the only instrument anybody in this borough has for releasing anybody from anything is a piece of paper with a name on it, and the one instrument she is not getting is a piece of paper with a name on it, because the absence of her name is the only thing keeping her out of it. That is the price, and it is a very small price, and it is exact, and nobody in this borough can do anything about it, including her.** AND THE SENTENCE GIVEN OUT LOUD IN THAT ROOM IN MARCH WAS ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE. It was correct. It is still correct. There are more than four, and nobody in this borough has an instrument for saying how many more, and the number is not going to be arrived at by adding them up, because the first of them is not on a list either and neither is the fifth and there is nowhere to put the answer. Nobody died and nobody was rescued and nothing was lucky and nobody was helped; the term in force that Friday morning is the fourteenth of them and it runs six days and stops at six o'clock on the Sunday morning, and there is an hour between six and seven on that Sunday when nothing in this city is in force at all, and there is no date in this city for that hour or for any of the others, and Jonas Mercer is not going to be in it and has not said why.

**Chapter 0143 — For Whom. Saturday the second of May 2015, from about ten past ten in the morning, the records office, second floor, the Old Ward; and a corridor outside it. Jonas Mercer asks NO QUESTION in that office or that corridor. THERE WERE SEVEN QUESTIONS OF A STRANGER'S IN THAT CORRIDOR AT ABOUT HALF PAST TEN, THE SHORTEST OF THEM FOUR WORDS, HE ASKED EVERY ONE OF THEM AT THE COUNTER AND NOT ONE OF THEM AT THE WALL, and not one of them was about anything, and he was given one thing, and the one thing he was given was a sheet of paper that is public and requires nothing of anybody. The building is open on a Saturday morning for that counter and it has been open for years, and nobody has ever written down why, and the answer is a car park and a canteen and a man who comes in at nine to do the photocopying and goes at twelve, and he is not paid for the Saturday and comes anyway and has never been asked to and would not know what to do if he were. **Jonas Mercer had come in on a Saturday for the first time in nine months, and had not come for anything, and there was a man at the end of that corridor who had come for something, and neither of them knew the other was there for the first twenty minutes of that morning.** The man is thirty-three, works nights, and has been on nothing since he was twenty, and had come in because a man at work had said there was a minute you could have, that it was free, and that you did not have to ask anybody for it. The counter on that floor is about eight feet long with a window in it that has never had a grille on it, and the woman at it is about fifty and has been at that counter for eleven years, **and she is not the woman who comes down to the ground floor at about ten and at about three, and the two of them have never been the same person, and there is nothing anywhere in that building which says so and there is not going to be anything.** She gave him the minute of the twenty-seventh of April, which went out on the Friday, is public, is unsigned, has no crest on it, and requires no person to do anything at all. He read it standing up in the corridor, on the side with the fire door on the wedge, and he is not a fast reader, and he read the first two items and stopped on the fourth and read the fourth again, and the fourth item is the only item on that sheet with a person in it, and the person in it has no name in it and is not on the agenda and is not a member and is not a departmental officer — **and in the middle of that item there is a sentence about what this floor is for, and the sentence names a class of people, and the class has no members in it that anybody can point at, and the man reading it stood in a corridor on a Saturday morning and found that out.** THE ONE LATTICE NOTICE IN THE WHOLE OF THIS CLOSE, at about eye height at the end of that corridor, and it defines nothing and requires nothing of anybody: THE RECORD OF THIS CITY IS AVAILABLE TO ANY PERSON, FREE, AND WITHOUT A FORM / ANYBODY MAY HAVE IT BY POST, OR CHECK IT AGAINST THE BOROUGH'S OWN PAPER IN PERSON / NO PERSON IS ASKED FOR ANYTHING IN ORDER TO HAVE IT, AND NOTHING ON IT REQUIRES ANY PERSON TO DO ANYTHING. The asking: `It says what this floor is for.` `It does.` `It says it's for a group of people. It doesn't say which ones.` `No.` `How would somebody find out whether they were one of them.` **And she said: YOU CANNOT. I could not do it for you this morning and I could not do it for you in a month and I am not going to try, and I want you to understand that the not-trying is the correct thing and not a brush-off. Here is why, and then you can decide what you want to do on your own time. The only way I could put a person inside that sentence is by making a new sheet. Anything new I write has got a box on it. A box wants a name in it. And the day a name goes in a box in this building, that person is on something, and anybody in this city can then ask that person things, and that person cannot say no and mean it, because being on it will be a thing that person did to themselves, in a room, with me, in about eleven seconds, and they will have done it because they wanted help.** `So the minute's got no name on it.` **The minute has got no box on it either, and those are the same thing, and it is not an accident and it is not an oversight and I put it that way myself in March and I have not regretted it once.** `Then what is the sheet for. If it can't say who it is for.` `It says who it is for. It does it in the only way there is, which is by naming a kind of person instead of a person. That is a real thing and it is a useful thing and it is the only way a public document is allowed to do it, and it is why nobody in this city can be written down as being helped by reading it.` **And the other half, the half that took her eleven years at that counter, given for nothing on a Saturday morning: A SHEET WITH NO BOX ON IT IS A SHEET THAT A PERSON HAS TO PRODUCE ABOUT THEMSELVES. That is what it is. It is not a sheet anybody can produce about anybody else. So the one document in this city that could tell you whether a person can be helped is the one that person has to bring with them, and the document is them, and there is nowhere on that sheet where them would go.** `So I cannot ask.` `You cannot ask, and I cannot ask, and there is nobody in this building you can ask, and I am not being kind to you, I am telling you where the wall is.` **And then he said the thing he had come to say, and it is two questions, and it is the question the minute was never written to answer. He had got as far as saying what was wrong with her, in a corridor, on a Thursday, and had stopped, and had said *somebody I work with* instead, and had done it without appearing to notice that he had done it.** `I am asking because of somebody I work with.` `Then I am not going to ask you who, and you had better not tell me.` `Right.` `Who is it for. How do I get to be one.` `No.` `It is a public minute. It is free. It is on the wall of a corridor with a notice about it.` `It is free and it is public and you may have it and keep it and put it in a drawer and show it to everybody in this city, and none of that is the question, and you know it is not the question, because you did not ask it.` `No.` **And the last of it, in one go: A SENTENCE ABOUT WHO A FLOOR IS FOR IS NOT A ROUTE TO A FLOOR. It is not a list and it is not a queue and it is not a way in. It tells a man like you that you are the sort of person a thing is for, and it gives you nothing at all to take with you, and that is not a flaw in the minute. That is what a minute is.** **AND JONAS MERCER WAS AT THE END OF THAT CORRIDOR, AT THE WALL, ABOUT NINE FEET FROM THE COUNTER, FOR THE WHOLE OF IT, and did not say one word, and nobody at that counter asked him anything, and the man never knew he was there. He had one question available to him in nine months and has not spent it since the twenty-fifth of February, and this was not the morning to spend it. AND THE MAN DID NOT SAY WHO HE WAS THINKING OF. He said *a group of people* and *somebody I work with* and he did not say a name, and he did not say where she works, and he did not say what is wrong with her, and Jonas Mercer has watched a great many people make a mistake in nine months and that is the first time he has watched somebody not make one: a person who is not in a record cannot be talked about in a place where the talking might be written down, because the writing down is the part that lasts and the talking is the part that does not. Nobody taught him that and nobody in this borough is able to teach anybody that, and Jonas Mercer is the person who made the first half of it cheap, in a room, in daylight, in nine seconds, on the third of December, and he has never met this man and is not going to and did not ask him one word.** Nobody was helped, nobody was harmed, nothing was put in any document, and there is no minute of that morning and there is not going to be one, and a corridor is the only place in that building where a conversation can happen without leaving anything behind, and a man in a corridor having a conversation is not an establishment. The man stood there a while longer with the sheet in his hand and then went down the stairs and out into the street with it, and it is a public document and requires nothing of him and he may burn it, and he put it in the inside of his coat, which is what everybody does with a piece of paper in this borough. **And that is the third and last of the three answers given in that room in nine months coming back at him from the outside of the building, and it has cost about half a hundred words and a piece of paper and nobody was helped, and it was correct, and it is not going to be improved, and it is not going in a minute, and it is not going on a wall.** The standing: nineteen entries and a blank line blank for a hundred and fifty-seven days, the card in the slot seventy-one days, the letter in the tray seventy-three days, the term in force the fourteenth of them, begun at seven on the Sunday before last, running six days, stopping at six o'clock on the Sunday morning, with an hour between six and seven when nothing in this city is in force and no date in this city for that hour, and Jonas Mercer is not going to be in it and told nobody and was asked nothing. All eleven refusals stand, none argued out, a request refused twice is one request and not two, and nobody was sent anywhere and nobody is going to be.

**Chapter 0144 — The Whole Of What There Is. Saturday the second of May 2015, from ten o'clock at night, a waiting area off a corridor on level two, Saint Orra west; and Sunday the third, from about twenty past six in the morning, four hundred yards from that building; and Monday the fourth of May into Wednesday the thirteenth, and on the Wednesday, from about ten past ten in the morning, the vestibule, ground floor, the Old Ward. Jonas Mercer asked NO QUESTION of anybody on any of those days and has not asked one since the twenty-fifth of February, and nobody asked him a question on any of those days either, and he was not on the agenda of anything because there was no agenda, and he did not chair anything, and there is no chair of that body and there never was, and HE DID NOT SAY A FOURTH ANSWER. **AND THIS IS THE LAST OF THE TWO THINGS THAT IS GOING TO BE WRITTEN DOWN IN VOLUME 03, and both of them are on the first two pages of this chapter, and the only reason either of them is written down at all is that a man worked it out in April, standing up, on a Saturday afternoon, and then wrote it in a pad in a shop at the end of a street, and the pad goes in a drawer in that shop and it is the only place any of it is. THE ROOM WITH THE FOUR CHAIRS IS NOT GOING TO CLOSE AND THE READING IS NOT GOING TO STOP. WHAT IS GOING TO STOP IS THIS.** THE FIFTEENTH, STAGED ONCE, AS `state/continuity.md` §0b ITEM 8 AUTHORISES: four chairs and a machine for hot water that has not worked since about August and a window with the corridor's reflection in it and a clipboard with a hardboard back and a sheet of A4 clipped to it and eleven rows on it with the first four written on in October by a man who then stopped, and the clipboard is on the fourth chair and **NOBODY WROTE ON IT AND NOBODY HAS EVER WRITTEN ON IT; IT IS NOT A LOG AND IT WAS NEVER A LOG**, and there is no rule anywhere that says anybody has to put anything on it and no person in this borough who could be asked to. Ezra Mbeki, in and out of that building since 1992, and Trevor Nunn, 58, a night porter, nineteen years, who is not on a roster because the roster does not exist; neither knew until about nine o'clock that they were going to be there. They read a term of six days, which began at seven the next morning when the gate went up and stops at six o'clock on the Sunday morning after that. **AND THAT IS THE FIFTEENTH OF THEM, AND THERE IS NO DATE ON IT, and there has not been a date written down on one of these in this city since the twenty-fourth of January, and there is not going to be one, and the reason is the reason there was never one: a person is not required to be told, and there is nobody in this borough who is required to write it down.** One of them said *same as last week* and the other said *there is*, and that is the whole of the conversation that has ever been had in that room. **NEITHER OF THEM HAS EVER BEEN ASKED ABOUT ANY OF THEM BY ANYBODY AND NEITHER EVER WILL BE, AND THE REASON IS NOT DISCRETION. THE REASON IS THAT THERE IS NOTHING TO ASK ABOUT. AND NEITHER OF THEM KNOWS THAT A MAN REFUSED OUT LOUD, IN A CORRIDOR, IN DAYLIGHT, ON THE SECOND OF MARCH, TO BE IN THAT ROOM, AND NEITHER OF THEM IS GOING TO BE TOLD, AND THERE IS NO WAY IN THIS BOROUGH TO TELL THEM, AND THAT IS NOT A FAILURE OF A RECORDS SYSTEM, BECAUSE THERE IS NO RECORDS SYSTEM.** They went home at about half past six. **And he was four hundred yards away, on purpose, and he had decided to be on the Friday, and the reason has not changed since the eleventh of April, and it has not changed this month, and there is nobody in that building who could be asked about it. THE GATE WENT UP AT SEVEN ON THAT SUNDAY BECAUSE TWO PEOPLE WHO ARE NOT ON ANYTHING HAD BEEN IN A ROOM WITH FOUR CHAIRS IN IT THE NIGHT BEFORE, and nobody wrote that down, and it has been like that since the twenty-fifth of January and it will be like that in June.** THE HOUR BETWEEN SIX AND SEVEN, STAGED ONE LAST TIME, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS SO ON THE PAGE: it is the hour after a term has stopped and before the next begins, it happens every Sunday morning, it has no name in this borough, there is no date in this city for it, and it is the only hour of the week in which this borough requires nothing of anybody at all. The last hour and a half of a term is not that hour; the first hour of a term is not that hour. In that hour four hundred and twenty women are not on the far side of a paragraph number, because there is no paragraph and no term; the eleven o'clock to half past one row is not empty, because there is no rota and no row and no board being read by anybody; the register has no nineteenth entry with a bulb out on it. **He had that in a pad in a shop at the end of a street, and he is not going to improve it, and it is here because the hour is being stood in one last time and not because it has got any better than it was in April.** He stood in it for about half an hour; a light came on in a car park on the far side of a building he could not see into and he could not tell what it was about and it was nothing. **AND THIS IS THE LAST TIME EITHER OF THOSE TWO THINGS IS GOING TO BE STAGED IN VOLUME 03, and it is said here, on the page, in a book, in a place nobody is ever going to look, which is the only place anything in this borough has ever been said twice.** The finding is four months old and has not improved: the hour cannot be scheduled without somebody being required to be in it, and the only instrument that keeps it unscheduled is two people who are not on anything turning up at ten o'clock on a Saturday because nobody wrote anything down. **He has stood in a corridor on a Sunday morning exactly twice and he is not going to do it a third time, because a third is a habit and a fourth is a rota with one name in it, and he is thirty-two, and he worked that out in April. And he has stood on that wet pavement six times since December by ten o'clock that night, and on the Sunday morning it was the seventh, and he did the arithmetic standing in it and it came out the same as it had come out in April: four hundred yards is not an answer, because a place a man goes to is the first half of a schedule. HE IS DOING IT ANYWAY. The reason he is doing it anyway is that there is no rule that could stop him and there is no person who could tell him to, and the whole of this borough's arrangement is that the only thing holding the shape of it together is one man not being caught out, and he is thirty-two years old, and he knows exactly what the odds on that are, and he did it anyway, and that is the last of it. AND THE HOUR DOES NOT NEED HIM. IT HAS NOT NEEDED HIM FOR FOUR MONTHS. THE ONLY REASON HE WAS EVER IN IT AT ALL IS THAT HE WAS NOT REQUIRED TO BE ANYWHERE.** And then nothing happened for eight days, which is what happens: **THE TERM THAT HAD BEEN IN FORCE SINCE SEVEN O'CLOCK ON THE SUNDAY BEFORE LAST STOPPED AT SIX O'CLOCK ON THE SUNDAY MORNING OF THE TENTH OF MAY, AND ANOTHER CAME IN AT SEVEN, and a room with four chairs in it on level two did that on the Saturday night before it, and there is no date on the night either of those was read on, and he is not going to give the second one one. SIXTEEN OF THOSE SATURDAY NIGHTS HAVE NOW BEEN REACHED. NOT ONE OF THEM HAS A DATE WRITTEN ON IT ANYWHERE IN THIS CITY, AND THERE IS NOT GOING TO BE ONE, AND THE ONLY REASON THERE IS NOT IS THAT A PERSON IS NOT REQUIRED TO BE TOLD.** ON WEDNESDAY THE THIRTEENTH OF MAY, AT ABOUT TEN PAST TEN: the board read nineteen entries and a blank line under the nineteenth, **blank since Wednesday the twenty-sixth of November 2014, which is a hundred and sixty-eight days today, and it is the only fixed point in that building, and entry nineteen is a yard light, and the maintenance is a bulb, and the bulb is out**, and it did not move once in the nine days and did not move once in the six weeks before them. The slot had a card in it that had gone in on the twentieth of February, which is eighty-two days, and nobody had taken it out and nobody had put it up, and the woman at that counter has not read it and is not going to. The drawer under the counter has no key on it and no list of what is in it and **there is no way in this borough to say how many are in it, including the woman who keeps them**, and there was a tray on top of it, and the two numbers anybody would want to add together were never added together in nine months and are not going to be added together in this one. Sixteen inches above the board, two conditions that do not agree have never been reconciled and were not reconciled on the thirteenth of May. The woman of about fifty came down at about ten, and neither of them said anything about anything that matters, and she said: `If you are going to stand there, stand on this side of the post, because they come round that corner with the trolley and it does not turn.` and then, when he apologised, `I am not telling you off. I am telling you about the post.` **AND THERE WERE PEOPLE IN THAT ROOM, AND HE IS NOT ON THE AGENDA OF ANY OF THEM, AND SOMEBODY SAID THE ONLY TWO WORDS ANYBODY SAID TO HIM ALL DAY: `You are not on the agenda.` `No.`** And then he said what there is. Nobody had asked him for it. Nobody had asked him anything since the twenty-fourth of April, and the man of about fifty-six had asked him three questions in nine months and had not asked a fourth. **AND HE WAS CAREFUL ABOUT IT: he was not going to make it a fourth answer, he was not going to say anything anybody could act on, and he was not going to say a sentence, because a sentence said in a room is a thing a room knows and it cannot be used, and he had known that since the eighth of April, and he was not going to be the one who taught that room it could.** He said what is there out loud in daylight in the order it came into his head, and he got about half way through and it came out badly and he let it come out badly, and part of the way through he noticed a woman near the door had put her bag down on the floor to listen and he went on and did not stop for it. He said the counter, and how long it takes, and that **it stops the morning she stops coming and starts again the morning she comes back**, and that it works because a person is standing at it and that person is not on anything and is not findable from any record in this city. He said the board, and the nineteen, and the line, and the bulb, and that the line has been blank for a hundred and sixty-eight days because a woman of about fifty said in December that a register which grows when nobody is hurt is a register somebody can hold over you, and she was right, and being right about it is the reason the line is empty. He said the card in the slot, and the drawer, and the two numbers that were never added, and that nobody can say how many pieces of paper are in a drawer under a counter and that includes him, standing four feet from it. He said the tray on the second floor with post on top of something in it, and that a piece of paper under two other pieces of paper is a piece of paper that has stopped being dealt with, and that nobody in this city is going to lift the post. He said the two conditions, sixteen inches apart, and that a contradiction is not a pause, it is a floor that is not level, and everybody crosses a floor that is not level faster than a floor that is. He said the compliance report, and the paragraph in it, and four hundred and twenty women, and a machine on level zero that a doctor bought herself, and the strip of paper that goes in a drawer in that room over and over, dozens of times a morning, by a hand that nobody has found and is not going to be. He said the board of nine seats, seven filled, two chairs empty since October and the end of November, no chair of that body and never one, four of the nine who have said they will stop if it turns into a job, nobody has stopped, and the board has not sat since the middle of January and is not required to. He said the six water points, and the winter price, and that nobody has been asked to pay any part of it, and that a man who turns a bucket for money is the most findable man in this city and nobody did that to him and nobody is going to. He said a Saturday night with no date on it, and two tired men, and four chairs, and a machine for hot water, and a gate that goes up at seven on a Sunday because nobody wrote anything down. **AND THEN HE GOT TO THE PART WITH THE PEOPLE IN IT AND HE STOPPED, in the middle of that room, in daylight, at about ten to half past ten, and everybody waited, and he did not go on, and here is the reason and it is the reason for the whole nine months and not a new one: THERE IS NO BOX ON A ROOM. A room can hold a sentence and a room cannot hold a person. If he had said a name in there that morning, then in about four years there would be a piece of paper in a kitchen somewhere with a name at the top of it, and somebody would have got that name from a man who was not on the agenda of anything.** So he said the other half of it instead, and it is the whole of what he has: **`There is a place in that where the people go, and I am not going to say it, and that is not modesty. There is nowhere in this borough to put a person, so a room is the only place a person can be mentioned, and a room is exactly the place where it does not last.`** Nobody wrote any of it down. Not the woman at the counter, and she is the only person in this city who can end a minute by picking one up and putting one down, and there was no minute on that tray at any point in the morning, and the sheet on it was a sheet. Nobody repeated it, nobody improved it, nobody has put it on a wall, the only place it has ever been is in a book at the end of a street that goes in a drawer, and it is not going in a document, and it names no person, and **NOBODY HAS BEEN ADDED TO IT AND NOBODY HAS BEEN TAKEN OFF IT**. **HE DID NOT WRITE IT DOWN EITHER, and the reason is not a better one: it is not his. He is not on the agenda, he is not a party, he is not a member and not a departmental officer, he has no grade and no department and no probation, and there is a woman of fifty-four in this borough who has stood in a shopfront since the fourth of July doing the one thing in this city that a person can be held to, unpaid, and she has not been told what it is, and he is not going to put himself in a room with her this week.** He went out into the street at about half past ten; nobody followed him, nobody asked him anything, and **the counter did not answer him about a gate, or a rota, or a paragraph, or a person, and it is not going to.** THE STANDING OF THE CITY ON THE THIRTEENTH OF MAY, and it is not a list, and it is not a roll-call, and it may not be minuted, indexed, numbered or put on a wall, and it names no person, and it was not made that morning and it was not made that year: a counter in a vestibule eleven feet by nine that answers in about eleven seconds and is not a service, cannot be given to anybody, does not scale, is not required to be kept going, and works because a person is standing at it who is not findable · a register with nineteen entries and a blank line under the nineteenth, and a bulb, and a hundred and sixty-eight days · a card in a slot, eighty-two days · a letter in a tray with post on top of it, eighty-four days · a drawer with no key on it and no list in it · two numbers that were never added · two conditions sixteen inches apart that do not agree · a public report with a paragraph number on it standing in front of four hundred and twenty women, and a strip going into a drawer in a room on level zero about forty times a day, and a hand that has not been found · seven of nine, two chairs, and no chair of that body, and the lowland not on the layer and not able to be seated, and the reason still that a node with no party is not a party and a place is not a person, and that reason is correct and is not going to be fixed by anything said in that room that morning · **a sentence in a file about what a floor is for, in the fourth item of a sheet that requires nothing, said once, in daylight, on the twenty-seventh of April, by a man who is not on the agenda, and not repeated and not improved and not on a wall** · a sentence in a pad in a shop about why a woman of about fifty decides what she decides, in about eleven people and in no document, and nobody is going to ask her about it, including him, including after all of this · a sentence in a room in March about what it costs, which is about four people, and there are more than four, and nobody has an instrument for saying how many more, and nobody is going to add them up · **a name in about eleven people and in no document, and a hole in a record, and the hole is the only honest thing anybody in this borough has produced about a person in nine months, and it is an absence, and it is the only one of anything on that list that nobody can use** · a reading rule in the inside pocket of a clerk of sixty-one who is on no board and is not going to be asked about it, and which is not going to be used again, and a copy of a record that went into a tray in a hall in Tideglass on the thirtieth of March in about four seconds, which is not a form and has no box on it · a street that is on no layer, and three positions nobody has argued out, and a refusal of a pump that was made in a kitchen on the first of June and has not been argued with since, and nobody went up that path, and no person on that street is described in anything that leaves it · a claims box that is closed and is empty · a sealed envelope in a drawer on a lower floor that has not been opened and is not going to be · **a finding of a borough safety panel of the twenty-third of April, stated out loud once, in a room, in daylight, on the twenty-eighth of January, by a man with no standing to state it, in a minute that requires no person to do anything, not resolved, not going to be opened again, of which only the consequence is left, and nobody in this borough has been made to live under that consequence yet, and nobody is going to be** · **a method that came out of that counter has gone out of that room three times and has gone on from each of the three, has touched a district, has a girl on a till somewhere who is not surprised by it, is in no document and cannot be produced by anybody, has gone further than he knows about and will go further, and nobody has written one word of it down, and nobody is going to, and he is the person who made it cheap and is not going to go and ask one question about it, ever, and the reason is eight words and it is in a pad and it is not going in here.** AND THE PEOPLE, WHO ARE NOT IN THAT AND CANNOT BE IN IT: a man of forty-four in a building in Gantry Ward who cannot be asked one question about any of it, ever, and that is not a protection anybody arranged for him, that is what he is · a man of forty-nine who can be asked one question tomorrow morning, about anything, for ever, by anybody, and has not been, and is not untouchable, and there is no argument in this borough in which not asking him is the right thing to do, and he is not going to be asked · a man of forty-eight whom nobody has asked and whom Jonas Mercer has said out loud, twice, in a corridor, that he is not going to look for · a woman of fifty-four at a shopfront · a woman of seventy-two behind a door, a great deal more frightened than she was in May and no more findable · a woman of sixty-one with a number of Wednesdays written on a card under a rubber band in a biscuit tin, which is a number of Wednesdays and not a number of people, and nobody has ever asked her about it · a man of thirty-eight who worked something out on his own in a kitchen in February, gave it away three times, told nobody, and is now findable to people he has never met and does not know it. **AND THE SENTENCE THAT HAS BEEN TRUE IN THIS BOROUGH SINCE THE SECOND OF JUNE AND HAS NOT BEEN REFUTED, and has not been refuted by anything said in that room on the twenty-seventh of April, and is not being refuted that morning: A PERSON WHO IS NOT COUNTED IS NOT A PERSON WHO IS HELPED. It is not a rule and it is not a wall and it is not a complaint. It is the shape of the hole, and nine months has spent itself going round the edge of it, and it is not going to be filled, and saying out loud what a floor is for did not fill it, and nothing in that building is going to.** AND THE LAST OF IT, and it is not a fact about anything: **there is an hour every Sunday morning between six and seven when nothing in this borough is in force at all, and there is no date in this city for it, and there has not been a date for one since the twenty-fifth of January, and there is not going to be one; and there is a register with a blank line under it that is the only fixed point in the building; and about eleven people a day walk in under two conditions that do not agree, and get a date, and go out again into a city that has not asked them whether they can afford to be found. And that is the whole of what nine months has produced, and it does not scale, and it is not going to be given to anybody, and there is no instrument in this borough that can say who is standing under any of it. And nobody in this borough has ever yet written down who is standing under it. And nobody is required to be told, and there is nobody here who is required to write it down. SO WHO IS.**
